This file is the single source of truth for *status*. `docs/design/TECHNICAL_DESIGN.md` owns *how*; the SRS owns *what*. Every working session starts by reading this file and ends by updating it (`CLAUDE.md` sections 1 and 8).

---

## 1. How to use this file

**Human team:** update the Status column after each stand-up. Move `NEXT` only when a task's dependencies are `DONE`.

**First time on a new clone (human):** run `sh docs/tools/setup-local.sh` (Windows: `powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File docs\tools\setup-local.ps1`). It keeps this file and the other local documents out of git and installs the commit hooks. Then `git status --short` must not list any of them.

**AI agent, every session** (shortcut: `/start-task`, then `/end-session`):
1. Read section 3 (Progress) → find the task marked `NEXT` in section 6.
2. Read that task's row plus its detail block in section 7 (if present).
3. Read section 4 (Decisions), section 5 (SRS Alignment) and section 8 (Open Questions) — these override the SRS where marked `APPROVED`.
4. Do the mandatory codebase analysis (`CLAUDE.md` section 2).
5. Implement, test, then append a session block to section 9 and update the status of the task and the `NEXT` marker.

**Status values:** `TODO` · `NEXT` · `WIP` · `PARTIAL` (merged but incomplete, has an open question) · `BLOCKED` · `DONE` · `DROPPED`.

A task may be marked `DONE` only when the Definition of Done in `CLAUDE.md` section 6 is satisfied. Never mark `DONE` with failing tests.

## 2. Project facts

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Product | CryptoPilot — Crypto Trading Support System (decision support + paper trading) |
| Period | 09/2026 → 03/2027 (26 weeks, 13 sprints of 2 weeks) |
| Sprint 0 starts | 2026-09-22 |
| Supervisor | Nguyễn Ngọc Lâm (lamnn15@fpt.edu.vn) |
| Team | 4 members: BE1 backend lead · BE2 backend · BE3 backend devops (owner column `DO`) · FE frontend/mobile |
| Architecture | Modular monolith (Java 21 + Spring Boot 4.1) + stateless AI Service (Node.js) — ADR-001, ADR-002 |
| Repository | `cryptopilot/` — structure in TECHNICAL_DESIGN section 4 |
| Branching | `main` ← `develop` ← `feature/T-xxx-short-name` |
| Local-only files | `CLAUDE.md`, `CURRENT_STATE.md`, `.claude/`, `docs/` — never committed (D-15) |

**Hard scope boundary (never violate):** no real order execution, no exchange API keys, no custody of funds, no investment advice.

## 4. Decisions

Decisions that bind the code. An AI agent must follow `APPROVED` rows and must not contradict them.

| ID | Date | Decision | Status | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D-01 | 2026-09-11 | Modular monolith + separate AI Service instead of microservices | APPROVED | ADR-001 |
| D-02 | 2026-09-19 | Backend runs on Java 21 LTS with Spring Boot 4.1.x, built with Maven and the committed wrapper (Boot 3.5 is out of OSS support, 4.0 ends mid-project) | APPROVED | ADR-002 |
| D-03 | 2026-09-19 | Package by module, then by layer; boundaries verified by Spring Modulith and ArchUnit | APPROVED | ADR-003 |
| D-04 | 2026-09-19 | AI Service is stateless; the backend is the only database writer | PROPOSED | ADR-004 |
| D-05 | 2026-09-19 | No MediatR / AutoMapper / FluentAssertions v8+; explicit handlers, manual mapping, Shouldly | PROPOSED | ADR-007 |
| D-06 | 2026-09-19 | Money, prices and quantities use `decimal` only; `double` is allowed for indicator maths only | PROPOSED | ADR-008 |
| D-07 | 2026-09-19 | UUID v7 primary keys generated by the application | PROPOSED | ADR-009 |
| D-08 | 2026-09-19 | Transactional outbox for notifications, email, push and AI requests | PROPOSED | ADR-010 |
| D-09 | 2026-09-19 | Matching engine reads the `kline_1m` stream (high/low) plus the mark price stream | PROPOSED | ADR-011 |
| D-10 | 2026-09-18 | Futures plans support isolated margin only | APPROVED | SRS BR-26 |
| D-11 | 2026-09-18 | One take profit per plan; no partial closing in v1 | APPROVED | SRS 5.4.1 |
| D-12 | 2026-09-18 | Simulated P/L includes trading fees and funding | APPROVED | SRS BR-36, BR-37 |
| D-13 | 2026-09-18 | An alert always belongs to a watched pair; the pair is added automatically | APPROVED | SRS BR-16 |
| D-14 | 2026-09-18 | Forum content uses soft delete; only posts can be reported (not comments) | APPROVED | SRS BR-47 |
| D-15 | 2026-09-19 | Project documents and AI tooling stay local (`.git/info/exclude`); commits, PRs, code and comments carry no AI attribution; enforced by local `pre-commit` and `commit-msg` hooks | APPROVED | Team lead |
| D-18 | 2026-09-20 | Database stack: PostgreSQL 16 + TimescaleDB 2.x, Docker image `timescale/timescaledb:2.30.1-pg16` pinned everywhere (dev compose, Testcontainers, staging); schema owned by Flyway SQL migrations with the TimescaleDB DDL in its own migration; staging is self-hosted Docker on a VPS, never a managed PostgreSQL | APPROVED | Team lead; TECHNICAL_DESIGN section 6 |
| D-19 | 2026-09-21 | Reference and bootstrap data is a versioned Flyway migration (`V3__seed_reference_data.sql`), not a runtime seeder: it is checksummed, applied once, and reviewed with the schema it depends on. Sample data is a **repeatable** migration in a separate location (`classpath:db/demo`) that only the `dev` profile adds to `spring.flyway.locations`, so production cannot reach it at all. Seeded primary keys are fixed uuid v7 literals (generation happens in the application and would differ per database); every seeded instant is the fixed constant `2026-01-01T00:00:00Z`, never `now()`; every insert carries `ON CONFLICT (<primary key>) DO NOTHING`, and a collision on any other unique key is left to raise. **Named exception (Q-25, 2026-09-28):** the demo subscriptions of T-108 start on the fixed instant 2026-09-28 00:00 UTC, not the epoch, so they do not expire before the capstone demonstration (03/2027) | PROPOSED | T-007, Q-25 |
| D-23 | 2026-09-21 | Repository shape: one repository per aggregate root, extending `Repository<T, ID>` rather than `JpaRepository`, so that `findAll`, `count` and `deleteAll` are never contributed to a table that grows with users — an unbounded read has to be written deliberately, and a paged one is what the listing screens will add. Reads declare `@Transactional(readOnly = true)`; `save` declares nothing, so the unit of work is the calling service's. A case-insensitive lookup is explicit JPQL over `lower(...)`, never the derived `IgnoreCase` keyword, because the schema's functional index is on `lower(email)` while the keyword generates `upper(...)` | PROPOSED | T-011 |
| D-24 | 2026-09-21 | Profile write path: `UserProfileRepository`, a repository for something that is not an aggregate root. The three options were weighed against D-21 and D-23 and this is the only one that bends a rule the design can afford to bend. **Rejected:** a cascading `@OneToOne` from the account, because reversing D-21 for one association turns "no JPA association" from a rule into a convention and brings a fetch decision with it; persisting through the `EntityManager` in the service, because it keeps both rules intact but puts two persistence idioms in one class, and T-014 has to *load* the profile to edit it, so the manager would spread rather than stay in one method. **The cost, stated:** "one repository per aggregate root" (D-23) now has one documented exception. It is safe only because it is forced — with no association there is no way to navigate to the row, so a row with its own table and its own key needs its own gateway or it cannot be reached at all. The rule still holds for anything reachable through its root. **For T-014:** the profile edit screen already has its gateway and needs no new one | PROPOSED | T-011 |
| D-21 | 2026-09-21 | Identity mapping: no JPA association anywhere in the identity tables. A profile is keyed by the account it describes, a device and a token name it with a plain `UUID`. Across a module boundary this is forced — `auth` may not map `user`'s entity — and inside `user` it is chosen, because nothing navigates one yet and an association decides a fetch strategy, adds a second way to load a row and becomes an N+1 the moment somebody makes it eager. Two tests guard the absence: no entity holds an association, and reading many rows costs one statement. An association may be added later with a reason, a fetch type and a test that states its cost | PROPOSED | T-010 |
| D-22 | 2026-09-21 | `created_at` and `updated_at` are written by one Spring-managed JPA entity listener from the injected `Clock`, never by a database default and never by `Instant.now()` inside an entity. A test that needs a known instant replaces the one clock bean | PROPOSED | T-010 |
| D-20 | 2026-09-21 | No credential of any kind in a committed file. The bootstrap administrator's password hash reaches the database as the Flyway placeholder `${admin_password_hash}`, supplied already hashed from `ADMIN_PASSWORD_HASH`; when the environment supplies nothing it falls back to `!ADMIN_PASSWORD_HASH_NOT_SET`, which is not a hash in any format an encoder produces, so the account exists but no password opens it. The address is `admin@cryptopilot.invalid` (RFC 2606), which can never resolve or belong to a person. `DEMO_PASSWORD_HASH` works the same way for the demo trader | PROPOSED | T-007 |
| D-25 | 2026-09-21 | Message arguments on `BusinessException`: a rejection carries the values its SRS message leaves a hole for — MSG09's minutes, MSG10's status — positional, rendered as text, and returned as a `messageArgs` property on the RFC 9457 problem detail. **Rejected:** the backend formatting the finished sentence, because that puts a copy of SRS section 5.3 in two repositories and makes the client's language the server's business; a named map instead of a position, because a message code plus a position is already a contract and a second one would have to be kept in step with it; a field per message, because every later message with a placeholder would change the response shape for every client. **The cost, stated:** the client now has to know the order of the placeholders of each message code, so SRS 5.3 is load-bearing at the level of the sentence for MSG09 and MSG10 — reordering the placeholders of a message breaks its display with nothing failing. An argument is always a value and never a secret, because it is returned in the body | PROPOSED | T-012 |
| D-26 | 2026-09-21 | "Remember me" survives rotation without a column: the successor of a refresh token is given thirty days when the retired token's own window was longer than the ordinary seven, and seven otherwise, so a remembered session stays remembered through any number of renewals. **Rejected:** a `remember_me` column on `user_token`, because it is a fourth column on a table A-10 has just widened and it would store something the row already says; carrying the flag in the refresh request, because the client would then be choosing its own session length after the sign-in decided it; letting every successor fall back to seven days, because a thirty-day sign-in would quietly become a seven-day one at the first renewal and nothing would report it. **The cost, stated:** the rule is a comparison of two windows, so it is sound only while they stay far apart — seven days and thirty. A later configuration that puts them close together, or that makes the remembered window the shorter one, reads the wrong answer and forces the column after all. `TokenProperties.refreshTokenTtl(boolean)` is the single place both windows are decided, so that is where such a change would be noticed | PROPOSED | T-012 |

| D-27 | 2026-09-21 | The role half of SRS 3.1.3 is enforced by the security filter chain as path rules, not by `@PreAuthorize` on controllers. TECHNICAL_DESIGN 5.3 said `@PreAuthorize`; this supersedes that sentence and 5.3 now records the split. Three reasons, in order of weight: the chain already ends in `anyRequest().denyAll()`, so a path must be named there to be reachable at all and an annotation could not open one — the chain is doing the work either way; "Guest" is a column of the matrix and no method annotation can express it, because an anonymous request has no method to annotate; and an area rule (`/api/v1/plans/**` is TRADER) covers endpoints that do not exist yet, so a controller added in a later sprint inherits the right rule instead of waiting for somebody to remember an annotation. **Rejected:** annotations per controller, because forgetting one leaves the endpoint unreachable and the fix under time pressure is to widen the chain, which is the failure this arrangement is meant to prevent; a mixed scheme with roles in both places, because two sources for one answer is the drift the matrix cannot afford. **The cost, stated:** a rule names a path, so a path that is renamed or misspelled silently stops matching and falls to `denyAll`. That fails closed and the owning task's own tests catch it, but it does mean the chain has to be read together with TECHNICAL_DESIGN 8 whenever the catalogue changes. `@PreAuthorize` stays for entitlement, which is not a property of a path; ownership stays in services | PROPOSED | T-015 |
| D-28 | 2026-09-21 | `CorrelationIdFilter` is registered at `Ordered.HIGHEST_PRECEDENCE`, ahead of Spring Security's chain (order −100). Until T-015 it had no order and therefore sat behind it, so every request the chain refused — every 401 and every 403 — ran no correlation filter: no id in the logging context, no `traceId` in the body and no `X-Correlation-Id` on the response. Those are precisely the responses somebody asks about afterwards. Nothing in the filter reads the caller's identity, so being ahead of authentication costs nothing | PROPOSED | T-015 |
| D-29 | 2026-09-23 | Lombok is used in exactly two places and made unable to reach any other: `@Getter` on an entity class, and `@RequiredArgsConstructor(access = AccessLevel.PACKAGE)` on a bean whose constructor does nothing but assign its collaborators. `backend/lombok.config` turns `@Setter`, `@Data`, `@EqualsAndHashCode` and `@ToString` into compile errors, so TD 1.3's prohibition stops being a sentence somebody has to remember, and sets `addLombokGeneratedAnnotation` so a generated member cannot move the JaCoCo ratio in either direction. **Rejected:** `@NoArgsConstructor(access = PROTECTED)`, which TD 3.4 permits — it is longer than the `protected UserAccount() {}` it would replace and it hides a JPA requirement behind an annotation, so it buys nothing; `@RequiredArgsConstructor` at its default public access, because five of these constructors are deliberately package-private and widening them to save a keyword is the wrong trade; `@Getter` on the four accessors that compute something (`isNew`, `isEmailVerified`, `isLockedOutAt`, `isUsableAt`), which are behaviour wearing a getter's name. **The cost, stated:** two classes keep hand-written constructors because theirs do work beyond assignment — `AuthService` derives `hashOfNothing` and `JwtConfig` derives the signing key — so "every bean uses the annotation" is not true and a reader has to see why. The annotation is only correct while a constructor stays a pure assignment; the moment one needs a line of logic it goes back to being written out, and that is a normal edit rather than a violation | PROPOSED | T-100 |
| D-30 | 2026-09-23 | A rule that both a request record and a service must read lives in the module's root package, not in either layer. `PasswordPolicy` moved from `auth.service` to `auth` because `RegisterRequest` has to declare BR-02 as a bean-validation pattern and a `dto` may not depend on a `service`. **The reason it was never caught:** `PATTERN` is a constant variable, so javac copies its value into the annotation and leaves no reference in the bytecode — ArchUnit reads bytecode, so `LayerRulesTest` passed 9/9 with the violation present, which the T-100 baseline of 674/674 proves. A rule that can only be broken invisibly is not enforced. `LayerSourceRulesTest` now states the same table over the import lines, and it was verified against a probe that reads only a constant: ArchUnit passed it, the source test refused it. **Rejected:** duplicating the expression in the request record, because the class exists to hold BR-02 once; moving it to `common`, which carries no business logic. **The cost, stated:** `PasswordPolicy` is now part of the `auth` module's public surface, and one layer table is stated in two files that can drift. The source test covers only the pairs inside `com.cryptopilot`; framework rules stay with ArchUnit, where the bytecode is the honest source | PROPOSED | T-100 |
| D-31 | 2026-09-23 | One factory makes every opaque token. `VerificationTokenFactory` and `RefreshTokenFactory` were identical from the package statement to the closing brace - same 256 bits, same generator, same URL-safe encoding, same SHA-256 digest - and differed only in the visibility of one constant. T-013 would have been the third copy. They are now `SecureTokenFactory`, and what actually differs between the three kinds of token - how long one lasts, what presenting it achieves, which rule retires it - lives on `UserToken` and in the use case, where it always did. **Rejected:** a third class for the reset link, which is the duplication the rule about reusing abstractions exists to stop; keeping the two and letting the reset link borrow the verification one, because the name would then be a lie at two of the three call sites. **The cost, stated:** a rename reaching T-011's and T-012's code, which is refactoring their files inside another task's commit. It is accepted because the alternative was to add to the duplication rather than to leave it alone - there was no option that touched nothing | PROPOSED | T-013 |
| D-32 | 2026-09-23 | UC-04 is two methods on `AuthService`, not a `PasswordResetService` as TD 4.1 listed. The two halves share the token table, the single-use rule, the digest-only handling, the non-enumeration shape of the resend and the bulk revocation of logout; a separate class would have to reach every one of them and would own none. **Rejected:** a class per use case, which would put the four token lifecycles in four places that all write one table. **The cost, stated:** `AuthService` is now 600 lines and holds UC-01 to UC-05. That is the cost of the seam being the table rather than the use case, and it is the right moment to split when a second table appears - the mail module of A-18, or UC-07's change-password, which needs the current password and therefore a session. Splitting it now would be splitting on names | PROPOSED | T-013 |
| D-33 | 2026-09-24 | Every access token carries a `sid` claim: the family id of the refresh token issued beside it. SRS 3.2.5 says a password change revokes all **other** sessions, and an access token that names only an account cannot say which session is "this one". The claim grants nothing and identifies a sign-in, not a person; the name is OpenID Connect's for a session identifier. A token without it (issued before T-014) is read as naming no session, and then every session is revoked — the stronger reading of the rule, costing the caller one sign-in. **Rejected:** sending the refresh token in the password-change body, because it puts the one long-lived credential into a second endpoint; revoking every session including the caller's and issuing a new pair in the response, because it contradicts "other" and turns a password change into a sign-in. **Extended in the T-014 follow-up:** the claim is also checked on every request — `SessionLivenessValidator` refuses a token whose family holds no unused refresh token, through `LiveSessions`, an in-memory answer cache of at most one minute evicted per account after the commit of every path that ends sessions. **Rejected:** a query per request with no cache, which puts a database read in front of every endpoint; Redis, which is not a dependency yet and would be the only reason to add it now. **The cost, stated:** one more claim in a token every request carries; rotation must keep the family, which it already does (TD 7.15); every future path that ends sessions (BR-06 in T-083) must evict, or its sessions live on for up to a minute; and the cache is per process, so a second instance needs the shared cache | ACCEPTED 2026-09-24 (team lead, by merging PR #3) | T-014 |
| D-34 | 2026-09-24 | Device tokens. A token is unique across accounts, so registering one decides by who holds it: nobody → a new row; the caller → the same row reactivated and seen now; another account → the row becomes a new device of the caller's (new key, new creation instant). **Since the T-014 follow-up all three cases are one statement,** `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (fcm_token) DO UPDATE` over `uq_user_device_fcm_token`, because the read-then-delete-then-insert it replaced failed two registrations arriving together (unique violation, or a delete of a row already gone) — `DeviceRegistrationConcurrencyTest` failed on the old code and passes on the new. Deactivation is by device key from the signed-in app (`DELETE /me/devices/{id}`, never the token in a URL) and by token from `POST /auth/logout`, whose optional `fcmToken` is deactivated only if it belongs to the account the refresh token belongs to. **Rejected:** moving the row to the new account, which would hand one account's device history to another; relying on the app calling `DELETE` before logout alone, because an app whose access token has expired would have to refresh first just to be silenced. **The cost, stated:** two ways to deactivate, and the logout body is now wider than the refresh body (`LogoutRequest` vs `RefreshRequest`) | ACCEPTED 2026-09-24 (team lead, by merging PR #3) | T-014 |
| D-35 | 2026-09-24 | A wrong current password on the Security tab is its own error code, `CURRENT_PASSWORD_INCORRECT`, 400 with MSG08 — not `INVALID_CREDENTIALS`, which is 401. A 401 on a request that carried a valid access token tells a client its session is over and would send somebody who mistyped back to SCR-04. Same message, different status | ACCEPTED 2026-09-24 (team lead, by merging PR #3) | T-014 |
| D-36 | 2026-09-24 | The Binance client is tested against a local stand-in built on the JDK's `com.sun.net.httpserver`, not WireMock. The task row and TECHNICAL_DESIGN 11.1 name WireMock, but it is not in `pom.xml`, and every case T-017 needs — a status, headers, a body, a delay, a count of requests — is thirty lines on the JDK server. **Rejected:** adding WireMock now, a dependency for canned answers; `MockRestServiceServer`, because it replaces the HTTP layer and so cannot produce a real read timeout or a refused connection. **The cost, stated:** a small test helper (`StubExchange`) to maintain; the payment adapters (T-067, T-068) may still want WireMock for signature fixtures, and that is the moment to decide again | ACCEPTED 2026-09-24 (team lead) | T-017 |
| D-37 | 2026-09-24 | Resilience of the Binance client is built from what the stack already has: Spring Framework 7 `RetryTemplate` for transient failures, and a circuit breaker of about thirty lines in `BinanceRequestGate` (consecutive failed calls open it for a fixed time, then one trial call). TECHNICAL_DESIGN 1.3 left the breaker to T-017 "after checking Spring Boot 4 compatibility"; Resilience4j's Boot 4 support was not confirmed, and the breaker needed here is small. The same gate enforces the weight budget and the 429/418 waits per venue, and it **refuses** rather than sleeps: a refusal carries the instant to retry at, and the job that called decides. **Extended in the T-017 follow-up:** a 418 is also written to `binance_ban` (V7) through the `BinanceBanStore` port and read back the first time each venue is used after a start, so a restart never forgets a ban and repeats the offence that lengthens the next one (2 minutes to 3 days); a 429 stays in memory. The rejection contract is one final `BinanceClientException` whose Javadoc binds callers: skip the run and reschedule at or after `retryAt` for RATE_LIMITED, BANNED and CIRCUIT_OPEN, wait for the next run on UNAVAILABLE, log a defect on REJECTED and MALFORMED, never loop or sleep in the calling thread. Only 5xx, timeouts and refused connections feed the breaker. **The cost, stated:** a breaker the project owns and tests itself; if a second integration (AI Service, payment) needs one, move to Resilience4j then rather than copy this one | ACCEPTED 2026-09-24 (team lead) | T-017 |
| D-38 | 2026-09-24 | Market model choices in T-018. (1) `MarketType` and `PairFilters` are published at the `market` module root, where TECHNICAL_DESIGN 4.1 already places `PairFilters`, because trading (T-035) will read filters through the module API. (2) A newly registered pair is INACTIVE with both markets off: BR-07 makes a pair visible only once an administrator enables it, so the exchange listing a symbol must not make it appear. (3) `PairFilters` refuses a zero or negative filter and one with more decimals than its column (12 for tick and step, 8 for minimum notional), because a value the database would round is a rule the system would enforce differently from the exchange. (4) `ExchangeSymbolMapper` leaves out non-perpetual futures (the SRS scope is perpetual only, and quarterlies share base and quote with the perpetual) and symbols with unusable filters, and maps any status but TRADING to "not trading" rather than dropping the symbol, so NSF-01 can flag it. (5) `minQty` and the precision fields are not stored: TECHNICAL_DESIGN 7.5 sizes a plan with tick, step and minimum notional only. **The cost, stated:** a symbol dropped by the mapper is invisible to NSF-01 — T-019 should log what it drops | ACCEPTED 2026-09-24 (team lead) | T-018 |
| D-39 | 2026-09-24 | NSF-01 as built in T-019. (1) **Universe:** the pairs already in `crypto_pair` plus `cryptopilot.market.sync.seed-symbols` (empty until Q-05); the exchange's ~500 symbols per market are never copied in, and a seed is created only if listed and trading, INACTIVE with both markets off (BR-07). Every registered pair is updated, enabled or not, so enabling one later starts from current filters. (2) **Absent from a market:** DELISTED if it had a status there or the market is switched on; otherwise it never was on that market (Q-14) and keeps no status. Never deleted. (3) **Idempotent** except `last_synced_at`, which moves every run on purpose, to show staleness. (4) **Runs** at every start (NSF-01 says "at start-up", not "when never synced") and on `0 5 0 * * *` in `UTC`, from properties; **disabled by default** and enabled in the `dev` and `prod` profiles, so a test context never calls the exchange. (5) **Caller contract:** RATE_LIMITED/BANNED/CIRCUIT_OPEN schedule one retry at `retryAt` (a new one replaces a pending one); UNAVAILABLE waits for the next run; REJECTED/MALFORMED are logged as defects. (6) **Single instance**, no ShedLock (TD 1.3). (7) "Admin is notified" is a WARN log (`NSF-01 FLAG`) until the notification module (P8) can deliver it. **The cost, stated:** a symbol dropped by the mapper leaves a WARN only; a second instance would double the daily read | ACCEPTED 2026-09-24 (team lead) | T-019 |
| D-40 | 2026-09-24 | CI build-and-test (T-009 slice). (1) The workflow is `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (TD 12 said `backend.yml`; TD updated) with one job named **`build-and-test`**, stable so it can be required. (2) It runs the local command, `./mvnw -B -ntp verify` — no CI-only profile, no skipped test, same JaCoCo gate. (3) No secret, no Docker login: Testcontainers uses the runner's Docker; `live` tests stay excluded. (4) Reports uploaded even on failure; a newer push cancels an older PR run, never a `develop` run. (5) Two test fixes the runner forced, neither weakening: the test database starts with `max_connections=300`, because the TimescaleDB image tunes the limit to the machine (`001_timescaledb_tune.sh`) and ten cached contexts of ten connections exceeded it on the runner; and the read-timeout test counts retries on the client (its refusal reports "after 3 attempts") instead of on the stand-in server, which never sees an attempt abandoned before it read it. **The cost, stated:** every new distinct test configuration adds a context and a pool of ten connections — keep test configurations few | ACCEPTED 2026-09-24 (team lead) | T-009 |
| D-41 | 2026-09-24 | NSF-02 depth and pages. NSF-02 names no depth, so: **1d 1000 days, 4h 730 days, 1h 180 days, 15m 60 days** — each beyond the 200 closed candles NSF-05 needs; ≈ 15,460 rows per (pair, market), ≈ 402,000 for the 13 demo pairs on both markets. Only the four BR-08 timeframes, each fetched natively; 1m is never stored. **Pages:** Spot 1000 (weight 2 at any limit), futures 500 (weight 2; 1000 costs 5, above 1000 costs 10 — measured 2026-09-24, TD 7.1.1). Initial load ≈ 442 weight on Spot and ≈ 832 on futures; hourly catch-up ≈ 52 requests per market. **Pacing:** the backfill stops at 50 % of a venue's budget (configurable, max 80) and continues the next minute, leaving room for NSF-01 under the client's 80 % gate. All in `cryptopilot.market.backfill.*` | PROPOSED | T-020 |
| D-42 | 2026-09-24 | NSF-02 targets every pair whose exchange status on the market is TRADING, whatever `pair_status` and the market switches say: a chart and the indicators need history before an administrator activates a pair, and activation stays manual (BR-07). NOT_TRADING, DELISTED and a null status (not listed there, Q-14) are skipped. **The cost, stated:** registered but never-activated pairs are backfilled too — with the seed list that is the 13 demo pairs, by design | PROPOSED | T-020 |
| D-43 | 2026-09-24 | NSF-03 connects to `wss://stream.binance.com:9443` (Spot) and `wss://fstream.binance.com/market` (futures). Binance split USDⓈ-M streams into `/public`, `/market` and `/private` routes; klines and mark prices are `/market` streams, and since 2026-04-23 the unrouted host only carries `/public` ones. **Checked live on 2026-09-24:** `fstream.binance.com/stream?streams=btcusdt@kline_1m/btcusdt@markPrice@1s` accepted the connection and sent 0 messages in 6 s; the same streams under `/market/stream` sent 23. The route is part of the configured URL (`cryptopilot.market.stream.futures-url`), not appended in code. TECHNICAL_DESIGN 7.1 and 7.1.1 said `wss://fstream.binance.com` and are corrected. **Also:** `kline_1m`, listed in 7.1 for matching and alerts, is not subscribed yet — nothing reads it; T-042 adds one stream name. Five streams per pair and market: 15m, 1h, 4h, 1d klines plus `@ticker` (Spot) or `@markPrice@1s` (futures) | ACCEPTED 2026-09-24 (team lead) | T-021 |
| D-44 | 2026-09-24 | NSF-03 streams a pair on a market when an administrator enabled it there (`ACTIVE` and the market switch, BR-07) **and** its exchange status there is TRADING — unlike the backfill (D-42), which also fills pairs not yet activated. Pairs are re-read after each symbol sync and every 5 minutes; a changed set replaces that market's connections and drops the old pairs' latest prices. **Latest prices** live in memory (`LatestMarketData`) until T-022 moves them to Redis; losing them loses nothing. **Snapshots** are written every minute at the whole minute from values at most 2 minutes old; a stream outage leaves a hole rather than repeating a stale price; the futures row leaves open interest and ratios to NSF-04 (T-023). **Closed candles** go through 4 ordered partitions (bounded queues, virtual threads) so the socket reader never blocks | ACCEPTED 2026-09-24 (team lead) | T-021 |
| D-45 | 2026-09-25 | NSF-04 writes open interest (and its value) and the long/short account ratios into **`futures_market_data`**, the row NSF-03 writes every minute, at the source's 5-minute instant — **not** into a table of their own. Why: SRS 3.1.5 defines FUTURES_MARKET_DATA as carrying "open interest, long/short ratio", the logical model and V1 already hold the five columns, D-44 and TD 7.1 step 7 left them to NSF-04; a separate table would be a 39th business table outside the logical model (an SRS alignment item) and would leave five columns empty for ever. How: each metric is an upsert that sets only its own columns; a missing NSF-03 row is inserted with the mark price columns `NULL`; the minute rows between readings keep the metrics `NULL` (no forward fill); only instants before the current minute are written, so NSF-03's `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` is not pre-empted. No migration. Read from the 5-minute histories (`openInterestHist`, `globalLongShortAccountRatio`) rather than the present-value endpoint, so readings are aligned, carry the value, and a downtime inside the source's month can be caught up (BR-10) **Amended 2026-09-25 (review):** NSF-03's futures insert no longer does nothing on a conflict: it fills its own four columns when they are all `NULL` (a row NSF-04 created first) and otherwise changes nothing; NSF-04's columns are never touched by NSF-03 (`43f65ab`). No reader in code assumes a non-null price today; future readers (T-024, T-027, T-029, T-034, T-055) must treat the columns as sparse | PROPOSED | T-023 |
| D-46 | 2026-09-25 | A funding settlement is due when `nextFundingTime − fundingInterval` has passed and nothing at or after it is stored, both values read from the source (stream or `premiumIndex`; `fundingInfo`). `fundingInfo` lists only adjusted symbols; **a symbol it omits has no interval and is checked on every run** (one request, within the shared 500 / 5 min) — never given a default, 8 h included (BR-11). Settlements without a mark price are not stored (the column is NOT NULL and BR-37 multiplies by it) **Amended 2026-09-25 (review):** a settlement is identified by its instant rounded to the nearest minute (`FundingTimes.normalize`), the one rule for every write to `funding_rate_history`; the next read starts 30 s after the stored one (`8844394`). An interval change between runs loses and doubles nothing; a lagging `fundingInfo` delays a settlement by at most one interval (`37369f1`) | PROPOSED | T-023 |
| D-47 | 2026-09-25 | The **code repository becomes public** and the **documents repository stays private**, so that branch protection (rulesets) is available on GitHub Free. Before the switch: gitleaks over the full history of every branch (8.30.1, 104 commits) found **no secret**; `secret-scan` job on every PR and on pushes to `develop`/`main` (pinned binary, checksum verified, `.gitleaks.toml` with no allowlist entry needed); workflows at `permissions: {}` with `contents: read` per job, checkout without persisted credentials; no `pull_request_target`, self-hosted runner or secret in any job. Known exposures the team accepts or fixes first: the personal e-mail in the author metadata of 104 commits (only a history rewrite removes it); references to the private documents by id (`TECHNICAL_DESIGN` in 137 files, D-/Q-/A- ids, ADR ids) — pointers without content; the supervisor's name in README; no LICENSE yet. After the switch: rulesets on `develop` and `main` requiring PR review and the checks `build-and-test` and `secret-scan` **Decided with it (team lead, 2026-09-25):** (a) ids of the private documents stay in committed code — citing a TECHNICAL_DESIGN section, an ADR or a D-/Q-/A- id is allowed, copying their content is not (CLAUDE.md sections 3 and 4 updated, docs `3c70a32`); **license: none for now** (all rights reserved) until the team chooses one. PR #10 (`chore/public-readiness` → `develop`) open, `secret-scan` and `build-and-test` green | **ACCEPTED** 2026-09-27 — repository switched to public by the team lead (checked via the API: `visibility: PUBLIC`); rulesets not yet configured. **Branch protection DEFERRED 2026-09-27 (Q-17, team lead): not dropped, enabled later**; no ruleset is created until then. Conditions that stay: CLAUDE.md, CURRENT_STATE.md and the docs stay local-only, and the `pre-commit` / `commit-msg` hooks that block AI attribution and local-only paths stay unchanged | Q-17, T-001, T-009 |
| D-48 | 2026-09-25 | **Service interfaces and DTO package split** (team lead, decided before the number of services grows, so that every later task follows one convention). Every `@Service` becomes an interface `FooService` in `service/` (only the methods called from outside the class) and `FooServiceImpl` in `service/impl/` with the original annotations; everything injects the interface. API records split into `dto/request/` and `dto/response/`; internal records (service, job, repository results) move to `model/`; client records stay in `client/`. A bean implementing a port declared in `client` or another module needs no second interface. DTOs may use entity enums only; `market.client` classes stay inside `market`. Enforced by ArchUnit. **Supersedes** TECHNICAL_DESIGN 3.3 "class, no interface unless there are two implementations"; TD 3.1, 3.3, 4.1 and CLAUDE.md section 5 updated. No behaviour change | PROPOSED | team lead |
| D-49 | 2026-09-25 | **Market data API shapes (T-024).** Five public GETs under `/api/v1/market` (SRS 3.1.3, T-015's raw-data rule): `pairs` paged with `PageResponse` in display order then symbol (in memory: 30–50 rows); `{market}/{symbol}/candles` with `tf` ∈ BR-08, `limit` ≤ 1000 (default 500), optional `from`/`to` (range ≤ 1000 candles, `to` never in the future), newest `limit` of the range returned oldest first, paged backwards by open time; `spot/{symbol}/stats` and `futures/{symbol}/stats` as two paths (not one polymorphic body), each block with the instant of the row that holds it plus `serverTime`; `futures/{symbol}/metrics` with explicit `from`/`to` instead of TD 8's `range=` (default 1 day, at most 7 days) and `availableFrom` per series (BR-10). Sparse series list only instants with a value; absent values are omitted, never zero (D-45). Limits in `cryptopilot.market.api.*`. MSG01 for invalid input, MSG41 for a pair not enabled on the market (all alike, BR-07) | PROPOSED | T-024 |
| D-50 | 2026-09-25 | **Market data stays public, futures metrics included** (team decision, closes Q-19). Every `GET /api/v1/market/**` endpoint — pairs, candles, Spot and futures stats, and the futures metrics (open interest, long/short ratio, settled funding) — is open to a Guest. SRS 3.3.3 says what SCR-11 *displays*, not who may call the API behind it; the access rule for the API is SRS 3.1.3 "Query Public Data" and T-015's rule that raw market data is public while the computed analysis (`/analysis/**`) is Trader-only. No change to the filter chain | PROPOSED · **Amended 2026-09-27 by D-60:** open interest, long/short and settled funding data (series and latest blocks) are no longer public; they need `FUTURES_ANALYSIS` | Q-19, T-024 |
| D-51 | 2026-09-25 | **Realtime payloads and hub rules (T-022)**, where TD 9 is silent. Payloads are JSON with decimals as strings and absent fields omitted: ticker `{market, symbol, sourceTime, …Spot fields | …futures fields}`, kline `{market, symbol, timeframe, openTime, closeTime, open, high, low, close, baseVolume, quoteVolume, closed, sourceTime}`, overview `{market, serverTime, tickers[]}`. Kline topics throttled like tickers (1 s) but one update per candle, so a close is never coalesced away; only BR-08 timeframes have a kline topic. SUBSCRIBE is admitted only to the public market topics, SEND refused; JWT on CONNECT arrives with the first user destination. Spring's receive-order option not used (it swallows the ERROR frame of a refused subscription); instead the inbound channel runs on one thread, FIFO, which keeps a SUBSCRIBE and its UNSUBSCRIBE in order — on the default pool they could swap and leave a subscription registered for good (reproduced 3/3 with 300 back-to-back pairs, fixed in `21d25f8`, 50/50 after). Cache flush every 250 ms (TD names only the TTL). Redis excluded from the health check (cache only, ADR-005) | PROPOSED | T-022 |
| D-52 | 2026-09-25 | **Indicator variants and test method (T-025)**, where TD 7.2 or 11.2 leave a choice. (1) MACD exactly as TD 7.2's table: each EMA seeded on its own (EMA12 at candle 12, EMA26 at 26), line from candle 26, signal seeded with the mean of the first 9 lines (candle 34) — not TA-Lib's `MACD` function, which starts EMA12 later so both begin together; the golden fixture builds MACD from TA-Lib EMAs for that reason. (2) RSI with `AG = AL = 0` (no movement over the seed or after smoothing) is 100, as the table says for `AL = 0`; TA-Lib answers 0 and many platforms 50 — Q-20. **Superseded 2026-09-27 by D-55: 50.** (3) Bollinger σ computed shifted by the window's first value, so equal closes give σ = 0 exactly and the bands coincide. (4) Tolerance `max(1e-8·|expected|, 1e-8)` (TD 7.2's 1e-8, made absolute near zero for the MACD histogram); largest observed 4.1e-10. (5) "Incremental equals batch (property test)" done with a batch reference written in the tests over the fixture and 12 seeded generated series (3 price scales, flat stretches), not a property-testing library (none in TD 1.3). (6) A duplicate is only the last candle again; any earlier open time is `OUT_OF_ORDER` and triggers a rebuild up to the latest candle taken, never setting the series back. (7) Rebuild from stored candles restarts the warm-up after a hole rather than computing across it. No indicator library in production code | PROPOSED | T-025 |
| D-53 | 2026-09-26 | **Two-axis model: style presets score the setup, risk profiles size it** (team and supervisor, closes Q-01 and A-07; full text, tables, reasons and rejected options in ADR-013). Definitions: component score = 0–100 score of one BR-13 component for one symbol + timeframe, no direction; style preset = primary timeframe + component weights, `SCALPING` / `DAY_TRADING` / `SWING`, versioned from `v1`; setup score = weighted sum of the components under one preset, 0–100, no direction; risk profile = `CONSERVATIVE` / `BALANCED` / `AGGRESSIVE`, for capital management and warnings only. Invariant rules: (1) the setup score depends only on market data and the style preset — the risk profile is never an input; (2) component scores and the setup score have no direction; (3) components are computed once per symbol + timeframe and stored, the setup score is computed at read time from the preset weights, no per-user score is stored; (4) a preset's weights add up to exactly 100%; (5) every setup score returned carries preset name + version; (6) preset weights and risk parameters live in configuration, changeable without code; (7) no user customisation of weights or risk parameters in the MVP (later, with Q-04). Risk profiles v1 (CONSERVATIVE / BALANCED / AGGRESSIVE): risk per trade 0.5 / 1 / 2 % of capital; minimum setup score to alert 75 / 65 / 55; maximum futures leverage 3x / 5x / 10x; maximum total open risk 2 / 4 / 6 % of capital; no profile above the AGGRESSIVE values; new users BALANCED; profile set by a 5–7 question onboarding questionnaire (capacity to bear losses, attitude to risk), changeable by the user with a warning when switching to AGGRESSIVE. Presets v1: SCALPING 5m (15m in v1: BR-08 ingests 15m, 1h, 4h, 1d only), DAY_TRADING 1h, SWING 4h; weights T/Mo/V/L(/D) Spot · Futures — SCALPING 20/40/25/15 · 15/35/20/10/20, DAY_TRADING 35/30/15/20 · 30/25/15/15/15 (the BR-13 weights), SWING 40/20/10/30 · 35/15/10/25/15; v1 values chosen from experience, adjusted through configuration. A-07: no direction, no `setup_bias` anywhere in v1. Default style preset for a user who has not chosen one and for any read without a user: DAY_TRADING (its 1h keeps the "1h setup score" of SRS 3.4.1 true). **Amended 2026-09-26:** (a) trend, momentum and level are directional and read LONG-only on Spot (BR-21; a strong down-trend or a close just under resistance scores low) and `max(LONG, SHORT)` on Futures; volume and derivatives are one formula on both markets (TD 7.4, worked example); (b) `dominant_side` LONG / SHORT / NEUTRAL, Futures only, the winning side of the trend component, computed at read time beside the score, never in the score, never stored — accompanying information that does not contradict A-07; (c) one style field: the existing `trading_style` selects the preset (SCALPING → SCALPING, DAY → DAY_TRADING, SWING → SWING, POSITION → SWING until a POSITION preset exists, not set → DAY_TRADING); no second field; enum rename or mapping class is T-103's choice; (d) BR-29 OVERSIZED_POSITION and HIGH_LEVERAGE thresholds come from the user's risk profile, replacing the seeded 2 % / 20x (hard ceiling 10x), done in T-037 with `SeedDataTest`; (e) the minimum setup score to alert is in configuration, no feature uses it in v1. Spec changes: A-34; TD 7.4, 7.5 | APPROVED | ADR-013, Q-01, A-07, A-34 |

| D-63 | 2026-09-27 | **Counting against a plan limit** (team lead). A task that calls `EntitlementApi.requireWithinLimit` (or `checkDailyQuota`) counts the Trader's existing items and inserts the new one **in the same transaction, under a per-user lock**, so two concurrent requests cannot both pass at `max − 1`. The lock is `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext(user_id::text))`, taken before the count — the per-user mutex TD 7.12 already uses for the AI quota, chosen because it touches no other module's table; a `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` on `user_account` would lock the `user` module's row and is not used. The count covers exactly what the limit names (ACTIVE plans and open positions, watchlist items, ACTIVE alerts, today's questions). Each such task has a concurrency test: two parallel requests at `max − 1`, exactly one succeeds. Applies to T-039, T-053, T-054, T-075 and to resuming a paused alert (T-054) | APPROVED | D-61, TD 7.12, T-039, T-053, T-054, T-075 |

| D-69 | 2026-10-01 | **What the plan checks and how (T-038).** (1) BR-21/BR-22/BR-30 stay in `PositionSizeCalculator`; the aggregate delegates by asking it again (`calculate(input) instanceof RiskInputRejected`) — the comparisons are never written twice. **Amended 2026-10-01 (PR #40 review):** a rejection is reachable by a Trader (a MARKET plan whose last price passed the stop by activation, a quantity below the step size), so it is `FieldValidationException` — `VALIDATION_FAILED` 400 MSG01 with `errors` field → MSG01/MSG15/MSG16, the shape of a failed request body — not an `IllegalArgumentException` (500); a calculation for other values in (2) stays `IllegalArgumentException`, a programming error. (2) `activate` takes the plan calculated again and refuses one for other values (`IllegalArgumentException`); only a MARKET entry price may move, and the new one is stored with the new snapshot. (3) Everything is checked before anything changes: a refused activation leaves status, snapshot and warnings as they were (the prompt's order was replace-then-throw; same result after rollback, but the aggregate stays consistent in memory). (4) `expiresAt` not after `now` at activation → `VALIDATION_FAILED` (400, MSG01); a draft may hold any expiry. (5) `expire(now)` expires at `expiresAt == now`; before it, or without an expiry, `IllegalPlanStateException`. (6) User and pair are fixed at creation; changing the pair means a new plan. (7) Spot stores `leverage`, `margin_mode`, `initial_margin`, the bracket rate and the liquidation price as NULL. (8) The snapshot is stored at the column scales (`PlanSnapshot.of`) | PROPOSED | T-038, BR-21, BR-22, BR-31, BR-32 |

| D-77 | 2026-10-03 | **No fill from a candle that opened before the activation (answers Q-33).** A `kline_1m` update carries the high and low of the whole forming minute, so a plan activated in the middle of a minute could otherwise be filled by a price traded before it existed. The engine skips a range for a plan when the range's candle opened before the plan's `activated_at` (`PriceRange.from` < `activated_at`; a merged range keeps its earliest candle time); the entry stays in the books and the next candle can fill it. A candle opening exactly at `activated_at` may fill. MARKET entries are not affected (filled at activation, BR-33). The brief's wording "before the activated_at minute open" is read as D-77 states it: with the minute's open the same-minute candle would not be skipped, which the required test (mid-minute activation: same minute no fill, next minute fills) contradicts. Trade-off: prices of the rest of the activation minute are not used; the activation itself fills a LIMIT already reached by the last price (BR-33) | ACCEPTED 2026-10-03 (team lead) | T-043, BR-33, Q-33 |
| D-78 | 2026-10-03 | **`executed_at` of a fill from the matching engine is the open time of the kline that reached the entry, not the arrival time of the update.** Live processing sees a minute as many cumulative updates, a replay after downtime sees it as one closed candle; keyed by the candle's open time, both give the same `executed_at`, which the T-043 acceptance test requires (replay equals live). Consequence for the queue policy of TD 10: pending updates of a pair are merged only within one candle (same open time), never across minutes, so each fill keeps its own candle's time. A fill at activation (MARKET, or LIMIT already reached) keeps the activation instant | ACCEPTED 2026-10-03 (team lead) | T-043, BR-33, NSF-07, ADR-011 |

Rationale: user intent at real time — the Trader decided on what the system showed then, and a replay must not reverse it with a past price. A replay that fills the plan before the cancel commits wins as usual (the cancel answers MSG43). Plan expiry is not treated the same way: NSF-09 (T-046) must not expire a plan of a pair whose replay is in progress | ACCEPTED 2026-10-03 (team lead) | T-043, T-046, UC-19, NSF-07, NSF-09 |

| D-87 | 2026-10-07 | **Alert engine shape (T-055, PRICE).** The engine keeps one in-memory ordered book per (market, pair) — one sorted map per condition — fed by the close of the `kline_1m` updates (`MinuteKline.close`, the last traded price; no extra stream). The stream thread only stores the pair's latest price and queues the pair once in its partition (`cryptopilot.alert.partitions`, bounded queue; a full queue never blocks, the pair waits outside it with only its latest price and a WARN is logged at most once a minute). A pair is evaluated at most once per `throttle` (1 s, SRS 3.4.4) on its latest price. A book exists for every streamed pair, so a cross has a previous price as soon as an alert is added. Without a previous price (just loaded, or forgotten after a reconnection older than `staleness`, 5 min) a cross does not fire. Off in the base profile, on in dev and prod. Books are loaded at start and refreshed after each committed alert change (`AlertsChanged`, AFTER_COMMIT); INDICATOR / OPEN_INTEREST_CHANGE / FUNDING_RATE are not evaluated yet. A cross that happens while the stream is disconnected is not replayed to the alert engine (`MarketApi#closedMinuteKlines` is not wired into it): an accepted limitation, moved to T-111 (2026-10-14: it touches the AlertEngine core and BE1's market ingestion, so it is separated from T-110 to limit regression risk; until T-111 is done, alerts may be missed during stream disconnects or restarts). Note 2026-10-14 (PR #56 review, point 1): the NSF-02 backfill does not publish `CandleClosed`, so a backfilled candle produces no `IndicatorsComputed` and no late indicator alert today; the replay task must keep that true or decide explicitly to fire late | ACCEPTED 2026-10-07 (project lead) | T-055, NSF-06, TD 7.9 |

GREATER_THAN / LESS_THAN are strict and fire on every evaluation while they hold; the trigger mode limits how often: ONCE once (then TRIGGERED), ONCE_PER_BAR once per 1h candle (BR-19 fixes 1h for PRICE), EVERY_TIME once the cooldown has passed (exactly the cooldown is enough). The cooldown applies to EVERY_TIME only. A price stamped at or before the last accepted one is dropped | ACCEPTED 2026-10-07 (project lead) | T-055, BR-19, BR-20 |
| D-89 | 2026-10-07 | **The database decides a trigger (T-055).** The engine decides in memory, then one conditional `UPDATE` records the trigger only if the row is still ACTIVE, PRICE, not expired, past its cooldown / outside its last bar, **and at the version the engine read** (an edited rule never fires on its old threshold); `updated_at = greatest(updated_at, now)` keeps `ck_alert_expiry_window`. Only the call that changed the row publishes `AlertTriggered`, in the same transaction (a rollback publishes nothing). A trigger the database refuses makes the engine read the alert again. | D-91 | 2026-10-12 | **Paper LIMIT fills: at once on each market trade, in queue order like Binance (TR-05).** The paper engine fills a waiting LIMIT order on the `@aggTrade` trade that reaches it as soon as that trade arrives (no 500 ms / 1 s window; the user: "khớp lệnh thì phải ngay tức thì"), at the order's own price as a maker, and records the fill at that trade's time. Trades that arrive while fills are being stored are merged per pair (lowest and highest price with their times, volume by price), so no price is skipped. At exactly the order's price, `limit-fill-mode` QUEUE (the default; the user: "tham khảo cách xử lí của binance") follows price-time priority: the order starts behind the quantity depth20 shows at its price when it starts waiting; takers selling at that price consume it for a BUY (buying, for a SELL), so a BUY at 80000 fills once more than that quantity was sold at 80000; a taker of the other side at that price fills it at once (no exchange order of its side was left there); a price through it (79999 for that BUY) always fills it. A queue not known (beyond depth20, or rebuilt at a restart) is read from depth20 when a trade first reaches the price. Paper orders do not queue behind each other. CROSS and TOUCH remain settings. Forming `kline_1m` updates fill only after `trade-silence` (5 s) without a trade of the pair; closed candles still fill (at their open time, D-78 unchanged for candles), move the watermark and drive the replay. Refines Q-T8 (CROSS was the plan default) and answers the TR-05 half of Q-38. Known limits: cancellations ahead of the order do not shorten its queue (it may fill a little later than on the exchange); one fill per order (partial fills by trade volume stay P2). | APPROVED (user, 2026-10-12) | `2f73c5b`, `af76d64`; plan Q-T6, Q-T8 |
| D-92 | 2026-10-13 | **Paper orders that execute on arrival take the book like Binance (TR-06).** A MARKET order, and a LIMIT order that crosses the book, walk the other side of `@depth20` best level first: one fill per level at that level's price, average price = VWAP (Q-T7). Without a depth the best bid/ask (`@bookTicker`) is one level; a pair without the trading streams keeps the last price, at which the whole order executes. When an order needs more than the levels shown, the rest executes at the last level's price and the order is flagged `DEPTH_EXHAUSTED` in `status_reason`; a LIMIT order does so only when its price lies beyond the last level shown. A MARKET buy by `quoteOrderQty` spends level by level on the step; a remainder too small for one step stays unspent, as on the exchange. A LIMIT order first locks its whole cost (the balance is checked before matching, as Binance does), then takes the levels at or better than its price as a taker: the rest waits as `PARTIALLY_FILLED` (GTC) and is filled later as a maker, expires (IOC), or nothing executes when not all of it can (FOK); a post-only (GTX) order that would take a level expires. The lock shrinks with each fill to what the rest needs (limit price x rest, rounded up), so all fills of a buy at its price cost what one fill would. `DELETE /api/v1/paper/orders/open?pairId=` cancels every working order of a pair and releases their locks (pair required, as Binance requires the symbol; an empty list when none works). Known limit: paper orders take nothing from the exchange's book, so two orders in a row see the same levels (plan section 19). Closes Q-38. | APPROVED (user, 2026-10-13: "miễn là nó thực tế và giống của binance thì làm đi") | `f4e66b2`; plan Q-T7, section 11.2 |
| D-93 | 2026-10-14 | **Indicator and funding rate alerts (T-110).** (1) NSF-05 publishes `market.event.IndicatorsComputed` (TD 7.9) inside the transaction that stores a closed candle's row, with the stored RSI14, MACD histogram, EMA20 and EMA50 of that candle and of the candle before it; the watchlist evaluates the RSI_14 / MACD_CROSS / EMA_CROSS alerts of the series in an AFTER_COMMIT listener on the same thread (trigger in `REQUIRES_NEW`). (2) The previous value of a cross is the previous candle of the series, not `last_evaluated_value`. (3) An indicator alert fires at most once per candle in every trigger mode and never on a candle older than the one it last fired on (`last_bar_open_time < candle`), which also absorbs a candle delivered twice. (4) FUNDING_RATE is evaluated once per `throttle` (1 s) on the cached predicted funding rate of the mark price stream; the level is percent (Q-30) divided by 100 exactly; the previous reading is kept in memory per pair, dropped after `staleness` or a gap without a rate; ONCE_PER_BAR uses the 1h bar (A-40). (5) Triggers use their own conditional updates on the read version (D-89); the T-055 PRICE query is unchanged. (6) Consequence (PR #56 review F-02): the indicator evaluation runs on the candle partition thread after the NSF-05 commit, while that thread still holds its connection, and each trigger takes a second one (`REQUIRES_NEW`); the pool is therefore sized explicitly, `spring.datasource.hikari.maximum-pool-size` = `DB_POOL_MAX`, default 22 since PR #57 (2 x 4 candle partitions + 2 alert partitions + 2 for the NSF-04 thread of the OPEN_INTEREST_CHANGE listener + 10 for web requests and schedulers; PostgreSQL max_connections 100); the test suite keeps 10 per cached context | PROPOSED 2026-10-14 (BE2, PR #56) | T-110, NSF-06, BR-19, BR-20, TD 7.9, D-88, D-89 |

| D-96 | 2026-10-18 | **Paper Spot on non-USDT pairs: cost valued in USDT at fill time, like Binance's wallet average cost (PT-F1a, review PT-R01).** (1) USDT value of a fill: `usdtRate(quote)` = 1 for USDT, else the current last price of `QUOTEUSDT`. A BUY adds `quoteAmount × usdtRate` to the base coin's cost (amount credited net of the fee, so the fee stays in the cost, as TR-13); a SELL adds `netQuoteReceived × usdtRate` to the quote coin's cost; the coin paid out always keeps its average (`reduceCost` on the quote coin for a BUY, on the base for a SELL); USDT is never given or reduced a cost. (2) A Spot pair is tradable in paper only when its base and quote are each USDT or the base of an ACTIVE Spot `XXXUSDT` pair; otherwise placement (order and order list) is refused with `PAPER_PAIR_NOT_VALUED` (409, MSG43). (3) Missing rate at fill time never drops or delays a fill: live `QUOTEUSDT` last price while fresh (price cache max-age), else the close of the latest closed 1m candle of `QUOTEUSDT` no older than 15 min, else the fill executes, the received coin's cost becomes unknown (`null`) and a WARN is logged. (4) Audit: `paper_fill.quote_usdt_price numeric(28,12)` and `quote_usdt_price_source` (`ONE`, `LIVE`, `CANDLE_1M`, `NONE`), Flyway V25 (named `_price`, not `_rate` as first asked: TECHNICAL_DESIGN 5.4 makes every `*_rate` column `numeric(12,8)`, too small for 60000, and the value is the price of `QUOTEUSDT`; Q-50); not exposed in any API response. A pair-level trade analysis (cost in the quote coin) is out of scope. Open points: Q-48, Q-49, Q-50 | APPROVED (team lead, 2026-10-18) | PT-F1a, PT-R01, TR-13 |
| D-97 | 2026-10-18 | **Realized, fee, funding and net PnL of a trade (A-02; BR-35, BR-36, BR-37, BR-38, BR-42).** BR-35 and BR-36 describe two different numbers. (1) `realized_pnl` = gross price result: LONG `(exit − entry) × qty`, SHORT `(entry − exit) × qty`; on LIQUIDATION the price result at the liquidation price, `(liq − entry) × qty` LONG, `(entry − liq) × qty` SHORT, with no cap; no fees, no funding. (2) `fee_amount` ≥ 0 = entry fee + exit fee, each = notional at the fill (`fill price × qty`, leverage does not reduce it) × rate; maker for LIMIT entries and TAKE_PROFIT exits, taker for MARKET entries, STOP_LOSS and MANUAL; `FUTURES_*` rates for Futures, `SPOT_*` for Spot (V3 settings unchanged). A LIQUIDATION exit pays no taker fee but the liquidation clearance fee `min(notional_at_liq × LIQUIDATION_CLEARANCE_FEE, MM)`, MM = maintenance margin with the bracket used to derive the liquidation price, at notional_at_liq (BR-27; Q-58), added to `fee_amount` with the entry fee; new setting `LIQUIDATION_CLEARANCE_FEE` = `0.01250000` (DECIMAL, BR-36) seeded by T-044; one rate for all pairs (known limitation, TD 7.8). (3) `funding_fee_amount` Futures only, signed + received / − paid: Σ over settlements while open of `mark × qty × rate`, a positive rate makes LONG pay and SHORT receive; null for Spot. (4) `net_pnl = realized_pnl − fee_amount + coalesce(funding_fee_amount, 0)`, derived, never stored (no column); one domain method on the journal trade model plus one SQL expression/view for statistics (TD 7.8). (5) Journal list and detail, Performance statistics (win/loss, win rate, totals, by coin/strategy/market) and AI trade summaries use `net_pnl`; WIN `> 0`, LOSS `< 0`, BREAKEVEN `= 0`; the detail shows realized, fees, funding and net separately. (6) Paper Futures, when built, books the same split (`REALIZED_PNL`, `FEE`, `FUNDING_FEE`, `LIQUIDATION_FEE`). No code or migration change: `trading_journal` (V1) already has the columns. SRS BR-35, BR-36, BR-38, BR-42 and the 3.5/3.6 wording edited in place (Version 1.0 Draft kept). Open points: Q-54 (liquidation floor at the liquidation price), Q-55 (PnL in notifications and the live position). **Amended 2026-10-18 by Q-54** (rules 1 and 2); **2026-10-19 by Q-58** (rule 2: MM bracket); **2026-10-22 by D-111** (rule 2: maker/taker by liquidity role, not by entry type: a LIMIT marketable at activation and a STOP pay taker). **Note 2026-10-24 (team lead), rule 3:** "Incomplete funding never blocks a close. The trade stores the sum of the settlements known so far, a flag that funding is incomplete, and the missing funding times. It is recomputed when a missing row arrives, and the net P/L is shown as provisional while the flag is set." The journal columns for this are T-048b; built in the domain by the T-045 follow-up (`FundingFee` known sum + flag, `NetPnl.provisional`, gap safety net, TD 7.8) | APPROVED (team lead, 2026-10-18) | A-02, T-044, T-045, T-046…T-052 |
