| D-111 | 2026-10-22 | **Trading Plan validity, entry deviation and realistic fills (GY-03, supervisor meeting 2026-10-07).** Principle: nothing agreed with the supervisor is removed; every mechanism follows how an exchange or a professional trading journal behaves and answers a concrete trader question. **M1 Entry types** LIMIT, MARKET, STOP. STOP is a stop-market entry for breakouts: a LONG triggers when the last price is ≥ the stop price, a SHORT when it is ≤; it fills at the trigger price, or at the candle open when the candle opened past it (gap); it always pays the taker fee. No stop-limit (it may never fill, which makes the simulation unclear). The stop price is stored in `entry_price`; a STOP without an expiry takes the LIMIT default of 7 days (D-71). A LIMIT that rested in the book fills at its own price, also through a gap (E7 kept). **M2 Entry deviation in R (Van Tharp)** `planned_entry_price` = the entered price for LIMIT and STOP, the reference price at the last draft save for MARKET. `deviation_r = d × (fill − planned) / |planned − SL|`, d = +1 LONG, −1 SHORT; positive = a worse entry. At activation of a MARKET plan: below `ENTRY_DEVIATION_CONFIRM_R` (0.25) it proceeds; from that value up to and including `ENTRY_DEVIATION_BLOCK_R` (0.75) it is refused with MSG51 carrying the recalculated quantity, RR and deviation, until the Trader repeats the call with `confirmDeviation=true`; above it, or when the price is at or past the TP, it is refused with MSG52. Optional per-plan `max_entry_deviation_r` (> 0 and ≤ the system block value; only stricter) replaces the block value for that plan; below the confirm value it leaves no confirm band. Risk stays fixed: the quantity is re-sized (BR-31). Every fill records `entry_deviation_r`: resting LIMIT 0, marketable LIMIT ≤ 0 (price improvement), STOP gaps > 0 (cannot be refused). **M3 Lifecycle** new terminal status INVALIDATED: DRAFT → ACTIVE → EXECUTED | CANCELLED | EXPIRED | INVALIDATED. An ACTIVE plan becomes INVALIDATED before its entry fills when (a) LIMIT: the price reaches the TP first (the opportunity has passed); (b) STOP: the price reaches the SL first; (c) LIMIT or STOP: the price reaches the optional `invalidation_price`, which at save (and again at activation, BR-31) must lie on the far side of the current price from the entry, so the price can reach it before the entry, otherwise MSG16 on that field (D-112); a MARKET plan has none. One candle touching both the entry and an invalidation level: the candle open decides when it already lies past one of them; otherwise, or when the open is unknown, invalidation wins (pessimistic, like SL first, BR-35). D-77 applies to invalidation too: a candle that opened before `activated_at` neither fills nor invalidates. Kline replay after downtime applies the same rules. EXPIRED unchanged (`expires_at`, LIMIT default 7 days, NSF-09). EXPIRED and INVALIDATED free the `ACTIVE_PLAN_MAX` slot and notify the Trader with the reason (MSG53, MSG54). INVALIDATED is listed in the Cancelled/Expired tab. At fill the setup score is recalculated; a drop ≥ `SETUP_SCORE_DROP_WARN` (15 points) adds a warning to the journal entry and a notification (MSG55); it never blocks (timeframe and preset: D-112). **M4 Learning data (journal in the Tradervue/Edgewonk style)** stored at fill: `planned_entry_price`, `entry_deviation_r`, the entry liquidity role, the planned `risk_amount`, `setup_score_at_plan`, `setup_score_at_fill`, a snapshot of RSI14, EMA20/50/200 and MACD; stored while open: the most adverse and the most favourable price (MAE/MFE, Sweeney 1996), from the 1-minute ranges the engine already reads. Derived, never stored (like `net_pnl`, D-97): R-multiple = `net_pnl / risk_amount` (the realized R of BR-42), expectancy in R = mean R, average deviation, cost of late entry = Σ `entry_deviation_r`, MAE/MFE in R. Read by the dashboard (T-051, T-052) and the AI context (T-074). **E1** fees by liquidity role, not by order type: maker = the order rested and was then reached (waiting LIMIT, TP); taker = executed against the market (MARKET, STOP, LIMIT marketable at activation, SL, manual close); liquidation = clearance fee as D-97. Amends D-97 rule 2; BR-36 edited in place. **E2/E3** verified 2026-10-22 (the futures `kline_1m` stream is subscribed and its `c` is the last trade price, but it never reached the price cache, so a Futures LIMIT never filled at activation): the Futures last price is the close of the latest futures `kline_1m` update; it is used for Futures MARKET fills, marketable-LIMIT checks at activation and STOP triggers; the mark price stays for liquidation and unrealized PnL. Amends D-84 and A-41. **E5** `SLIPPAGE_BPS` (INT, default 0): applied against the Trader to taker fills only (buy × (1 + bps / 10 000), sell × (1 − bps / 10 000), rounded to the tick against the Trader); at MARKET activation the expected fill includes it, so the quantity is sized on the price paid; never on maker fills or liquidation; paper trading (D-86) not affected. **E6** 1-minute candles hide the order of prices inside the minute, so every same-candle rule is a stated pessimistic assumption (TD 7.7 simulation assumptions). This decision does not answer Q-61 (exits). References: Van Tharp, *Trade Your Way to Financial Freedom* (2nd ed., 2006); Sweeney, *Maximum Adverse Excursion* (1996); Harris, *Trading and Exchanges* (2003), ch. 4 and 6; Binance documentation on order types, stop-market triggers and mark vs last price. Verification: `../reviews/PLAN_REALISM_VERIFY_2026-10-22.md`. Source of GY-03: the team lead's summary (D-112, `docs/meetings/2026-10-07_supervisor.md`). | APPROVED (team lead, 2026-10-22); refinements approved and wrong-side message set by D-112 (2026-10-22) | GY-03, A-48, BR-32, BR-33, BR-36, BR-68, BR-69, BR-70, NSF-09, MSG51…MSG55, TD 7.7, 7.8, TP-0, TP-A, TP-B, TP-C, T-046, T-048, T-051, T-074, T-113 |
| D-112 | 2026-10-22 | **Setup score of a plan, activation refusals, D-111 refinements, wrong-side price message and the GY-03 source (answers Q-71, Q-72, Q-73).** **Q-71 Setup score of a plan.** A plan has a required `analysis_timeframe`, one of the BR-08 timeframes (15m, 1h, 4h, 1d). Its default follows the Trader's current style preset: SCALPING 15m, DAY_TRADING 1h, SWING 4h, with the trading style mapped as BR-13 (POSITION → SWING, no style → DAY_TRADING); the Trader may change it. Existing plans are backfilled from their owner's style preset the same way. "At plan" means at activation, not at the last draft save: the setup score is computed with the Trader's preset at that moment, from the components of the latest closed candle of `analysis_timeframe`, and stored as `setup_score_at_plan` with `setup_score_preset` (name and version, e.g. `DAY_TRADING v1`). At the fill, `setup_score_at_fill` uses the stored preset and version, and `fill_indicators` (RSI14, EMA20/50/200, MACD) come from the latest closed candle of the same timeframe; a forming candle is never used; TD 7.4 formulas and weights are unchanged. `setup_score_drop_warned` is set when `setup_score_at_plan − setup_score_at_fill ≥ SETUP_SCORE_DROP_WARN` (15): journal warning and MSG55, never blocks; when either score is null there is no comparison, no flag and no MSG55. The `setup_score_*`, `setup_score_preset` and `fill_indicators` columns are nullable. **Q-72 Activation refusals** (Activate and Save and Activate; last price, never mark price): (1) a STOP whose trigger the last price has already reached (LONG last ≥ stop, SHORT last ≤ stop) is refused on `entryPrice`; it is never converted to MARKET, the Trader chooses MARKET, which runs the BR-69 check; (2) a LIMIT whose TP, a STOP whose SL, or a LIMIT or STOP whose `invalidation_price` the last price has already reached is refused on that field; no plan becomes ACTIVE only to be invalidated at once; "reached" uses the inclusive bounds of the books (TD 7.7). **Wrong-side price message.** MSG15 stays for values outside a numeric range only. Every wrong-side price error uses MSG16 ("For {direction} positions: {rule}.") on the field concerned, with `{rule}` stating the violated condition against the current price: the two cases above and `invalidation_price` on the wrong side at save and at activation (D-111). No new message code. **D-111 refinements, approved:** (R1) INVALIDATED is listed in the Cancelled/Expired tab with its status badge; (R2) a STOP without an expiry gets 7 days as LIMIT (D-71), an entered expiry is kept; (R3) D-77 applies to invalidation: a candle opened before `activated_at` neither fills nor invalidates; (R4) an unknown candle open counts as "invalidation wins" (as SL first, BR-35) and each case is logged with plan, pair and candle open time; (R5) the entry candle adds only its adverse extreme to MAE and nothing to MFE, also when filled at the open; the exit candle is clamped at the exit price; (R6) slippage applies to every taker fill, a STOP after its trigger included, rounded to the tick against the Trader; when the candle opened past the trigger the open is the reference before slippage; at MARKET activation the expected fill includes it, so quantity is sized on the price paid; paper trading (D-86) is not affected; (R7) effective block = min(`max_entry_deviation_r`, `ENTRY_DEVIATION_BLOCK_R`); a per-plan value below `ENTRY_DEVIATION_CONFIRM_R` removes the confirm step; a per-plan value above `ENTRY_DEVIATION_BLOCK_R` is refused at save (MSG15). **Q-73 GY-03 source.** There are no written minutes of the 2026-10-07 meeting; GY-03 and the BTC example are the team lead's summary of the supervisor's feedback, not the supervisor's wording, and D-111 is the team's design decision approved by the team lead. Record: `docs/meetings/2026-10-07_supervisor.md`. **TP-A dependency.** TP-A (schema, enums, domain, API) no longer depends on T-045; TP-0 keeps T-045 and TP-A. **Note 2026-10-23 (team lead, answers Q-77; replaces the earlier answer of the same day):** the analysis screen scores the setup with the preset's own timeframe (SCALPING 15m, DAY_TRADING 1h, SWING 4h) whatever chart timeframe is open (D-53), so the score a plan stores is the one the Trader saw on the Setup Score card: at activation, the latest closed candle of the preset's own timeframe weighted with the Trader's preset at that moment, not the plan's `analysis_timeframe`; this overrides "of `analysis_timeframe`" in Q-71 above for the score only. `analysis_timeframe` stays: it is the plan's chart timeframe and the timeframe of `fill_indicators` at the fill (T-048c). At the fill, T-048c rescores with the stored preset and version on that preset's own timeframe. The formula and weights are unchanged, and a preset's weights are never applied to another timeframe. Only that candle counts: when its indicator row is missing (an older row is the newest stored) the score is null, never a stale one. Its open time is not a column: it is the open of the candle before the one containing `activated_at` on the stored preset's timeframe, because the read is made at `activated_at`. When the read fails, the score and the preset are stored null, a WARN names the plan, and the activation goes on. In code: `MarketApi#presetTimeframe(style)` gives the timeframe from the preset configuration and activation calls `closedCandleSetupScore` with it. **Note 2026-10-24 (team lead, TP-0): slippage of the two entries filled at activation.** (1) MARKET: R6 holds for every calculation, not only activation: save, `/plans/calculate` and activation price the entry at the last price moved by `SLIPPAGE_BPS` (the expected fill) and size the quantity on it; its entry slippage is therefore already inside the risk and reward amounts, so the fee-aware panel (D-116) does not add it again to the loss at stop loss or subtract it from the reward. (2) A LIMIT the last price already reaches at activation fills as taker at the last price moved by the slippage, but never past its own limit (LONG `min`, SHORT `max`): a LIMIT never fills worse than its limit. Its quantity stays sized on the limit price, so its (capped) entry slippage is counted in the loss at stop loss and taken from the reward, as D-116 states. | APPROVED (team lead, 2026-10-22); note 2026-10-24 (team lead) | Q-71, Q-72, Q-73, D-111, BR-13, BR-68, BR-69, BR-70, MSG15, MSG16, TD 7.4, 7.7, 7.8, TP-A, TP-B, TP-C, T-040, T-048, T-113 |
| D-113 | 2026-10-22 | **Community read routes follow the session's shell (FE, applies D-99 CF-01a on the web).** `/community`, `/community/:postId` and `/community/members/:memberId` form a route group read by everyone: a signed-in Trader sees them in the Trader shell, a Guest (or anyone while the session cannot be checked) in the Guest shell, a locked or banned account the blocked screen, an Admin the usual denial. `/community/new` and `/community/:postId/edit` stay Trader routes. A Guest's like, save, comment, report or New post opens a sign-in invitation that returns to the page; the Guest header links Community instead of the access gate (News stays gated). The member page traces to D-99. ADR-022 | PROPOSED (FE, waits for the FE owner) | — |
| D-114 | 2026-10-24 | **T-082, T-083, T-084, T-085 and T-086 move from BE2 to BE3**: the BE3 member took the administration backend over and built it (said 2026-10-24). The other BE2 tasks keep their owner | APPROVED (user, 2026-10-24) | T-082…T-086 |
| D-115 | 2026-10-24 | **An administrator disabling a pair's Spot market cancels its paper orders, as the exchange's stop does** (BE3, T-085 review). After UC-45 takes the market out of service no stream feeds the pair, so a resting order never fills and keeps its funds locked, and a stop reaching one failed on a pair that is no longer tradable. Paper plan §6.2 already asks for the orders of a disabled pair to be cancelled with their funds given back; the 2026-10-23 note "an admin-disabled pair keeps executing" (`06da02c`) is withdrawn. Reason `PAIR_NOT_TRADING` is kept, which the web already words. Futures paper orders do not exist yet (Spot only) | PROPOSED (team lead to confirm) | T-085, TR-02, paper plan §6.2 |
| D-116 | 2026-10-24 | **Fee-aware risk panel (team lead decision 2026-10-23, TP-0).** BR-23 does not change: the quantity is still sized on \|entry − stop loss\| only; fees and slippage are shown and warned about, never sized in. Create, update, `/plans/calculate` and activation compute, store in the snapshot (BR-31; V34 columns, null on older plans) and answer as decimal strings: `estimatedEntryFee` = rate of the entry's expected liquidity role × entry notional (MARKET and STOP taker; a LIMIT the current last price already reaches taker; a resting LIMIT maker); `estimatedStopLossExitFee` = taker × quantity × stop loss; `estimatedTakeProfitExitFee` = maker × quantity × take profit; `estimatedSlippage` = `SLIPPAGE_BPS` on the taker legs (the entry when taker, the stop loss exit), in quote currency; `lossAtStopLoss` = risk amount + entry fee + stop loss exit fee + slippage of those legs; `feeShareOfRisk` = (lossAtStopLoss − risk amount) / risk amount; `netRiskReward` = (reward amount − entry fee − take profit exit fee − entry slippage) / lossAtStopLoss. The same fee of a fill (`FeeSchedule.fee`), liquidity roles and slippage as the simulated fills, no second fee formula. Funding and the liquidation clearance fee are not included, and the response documentation says so. New warning `HIGH_FEE_SHARE`, severity WARNING (never blocking), raised when `feeShareOfRisk` ≥ `FEE_RISK_WARN_RATIO` (`system_setting`, DECIMAL, seeded 0.20000000, read on every calculation); message after MSG17 states the share ("fees and slippage are 24% of your risk"). BR-29 goes from eight to nine warnings (A-50). MARKET entry slippage and the marketable LIMIT cap: D-112 note 2026-10-24. Vectors in TD 7.5. | APPROVED (team lead, 2026-10-23) | BR-23, BR-29, BR-31, BR-36, MSG17, A-50, D-111, D-112, TD 7.5, 7.8, TP-0, T-040, T-113 |
| D-117 | 2026-10-25 | **Paper trading joins the core model (PCI)**, replacing Q-T1's separation at the data level: the 12 `paper_*` tables become 6 business tables owned by the Trader (`user_id`) — `wallet_balance`, `wallet_ledger_entry`, `order_list`, `trade_order` (with `plan_id` → `trading_plan`, null for an order placed on the trading screen), `order_fill`, `equity_snapshot` — and 1 technical table `order_matching_watermark`. `paper_account` is folded into the Trader (an account is open once its INITIAL_GRANT entries exist; `position_mode` dropped with Futures paper), `paper_transfer` into the ledger (the two TRANSFER entries share its id and carry `client_transfer_id`; grant ref `ACCOUNT` → `USER`), and `paper_position`, `paper_funding_payment`, `paper_futures_setting` (never written) are dropped: a simulated Futures position is a plan and its journal record, funding comes from `funding_rate_history`. V34, one transaction with pre/post checks; `paper_account` and `paper_transfer` kept as `legacy_*` until a later migration. Both matching engines and the API stay as they are; merging the engines (plans placing `trade_order`s, Futures in the paper engine) is phase 2 with BE1. API: `GET/POST /paper/account` answers `{openedAt}` only; ledger `refType` USER replaces ACCOUNT | APPROVED (user, 2026-10-24); team lead to confirm | TR-02, TR-04, paper plan, `docs/design/paper-core-integration-plan.md` |
| D-118 | 2026-10-25 | **T-048 is split into three tasks** with the same Refs (BR-34, BR-40) and its "Done when" text divided between them: **T-048a** journal module, OPEN record at fill, open-position counting, position block in the plan API (depends on TP-A); **T-048b** exit wiring and closing (depends on T-048a, T-045, TP-0); **T-048c** the D-111 learning data (depends on T-048b, TP-C). T-048 is reported PARTIAL until T-048b and T-048c are DONE. **Note 2026-10-25 (team lead, PR #71 review): plans EXECUTED before T-048a.** They have no `trading_journal` row and are not backfilled: `GET /plans/{id}` answers `position: null` and the list `positionStatus: null` for them, and they do not count as open positions in `ACTIVE_PLAN_MAX` or TOTAL_OPEN_RISK. No shared environment holds such a plan (staging is not deployed), and the dev demo data holds none (`db/demo` has no `trading_plan` rows; DEMO-01's `load-wf01.ps1` creates only waiting ACTIVE LIMIT plans at 0.50 × the last price, which no fill reaches). Local databases are reset before the demo; there is no reset script, the exact command is `docker compose -f deploy/docker-compose.dev.yml down -v` (drops the volume `cryptopilot-dev_pgdata`; Redis keeps nothing), then `up -d`, start the backend (Flyway applies V1–V35 and `db/demo`) and run `.\deploy\demo\load-wf01.ps1` once DEMO-01 is merged. V35 is unchanged | APPROVED | Team lead, 2026-10-25 |
| D-119 | 2026-10-25 | **Journal module direction and the place of an open position's levels (T-048a; amends TD section 2 rule 5).** (1) `journal` may depend on `trading`'s public API; `trading` never depends on `journal`, so TD 2 rule 5 (trading records executions through `journal.JournalApi`) is reversed: `trading` declares the port `trading.TradeJournal` in its root package, with its records `trading.SimulatedTradeOpening` and `trading.RecordedPosition` and the enum `trading.model.enums.PositionStatus`, and `journal.service.impl.PersistentTradeJournal` implements it, as `market.OpenPositionCheck` and `user.AccountActivityCounter` are implemented by the modules that depend on them (CLAUDE.md section 5: a bean implementing another module's port lives in `service/impl`). The port is a required bean: a fill never commits without its journal record. (2) `openSimulatedTrade` runs with `MANDATORY` propagation, inside the transaction of the compare-and-set; `trading` computes the entry fee (`FeeSchedule.fee`, rounded with `Rounding.toAmount` as `TradeResultCalculator` rounds it at close) and the journal only stores it. (3) Open positions are read as the plan ids of the user's OPEN SIMULATED rows; `trading` counts them and sums the risk amount of those plans itself (`trading_plan.risk_amount`, the snapshot, never changed once EXECUTED). The D-63 count reads ACTIVE plans first and open positions second: a fill committing between the two reads is then counted twice (a spurious MSG27), never zero times. (4) New column `trading_journal.entry_fee_amount` (V35): the entry fee stays readable after the close; `fee_amount` stays null while OPEN and becomes entry + exit fee at close (T-048b), so the TD 7.8 net expression is unchanged. (5) Designed for T-048b and TP-E, not built: the CURRENT stop loss and take profit of an open position live in one place, the journal row's `stop_loss_price` / `take_profit_price` (written at open from the plan), versioned by the row's optimistic-lock `version`; a later change (TP-E) is a compare-and-set on that version, and the exit book reads only these columns. The ORIGINAL stop loss, take profit and planned risk amount never change: they are the EXECUTED plan's values (terminal, never edited) and, inside the journal, `risk_amount` (V32, written by T-048c); since nothing moves the current levels before TP-E, TP-E's migration adds `original_stop_loss_price` / `original_take_profit_price` backfilled from the current ones | APPROVED | Team lead brief, 2026-10-25 (T-048a points 2 and 6) |
| D-120 | 2026-10-25 | **Demo data on staging: a `demo` profile for the accounts, an API script for the rest (DEMO-01; amends D-62 (3)).** (1) `application-demo.yml` adds `classpath:db/demo` to the Flyway locations and nothing else; staging runs `prod,demo`, production `prod` alone. D-62 (3) said only `application-dev.yml` adds that location; now `dev` and `demo` do. `DemoProfileTest` asserts that base and `prod` never list it, and `SeedDataTest` keeps asserting it of the running context. (2) The WF-01 accounts `demo.free` / `demo.pro` / `demo.premium` are `db/demo/R__demo_wf01_accounts.sql` (subscriptions 2026-10-25 → 2027-10-25, the D-19 / Q-25 exception). (3) Pairs, leverage brackets and demo.free's 3 ACTIVE plans are **not** Flyway rows: pairs exist only after NSF-01 and a plan is only valid when calculated against a current price. `deploy/demo/load-wf01.ps1` (PowerShell 5.1 / 7) loads them from `deploy/demo/wf01.json` through the admin and plan API, writes only what differs (idempotent), and stops non-zero on the first problem. No trading code is touched: the plan service is internal to `trading`, so HTTP is the only path that does not change it. (4) Brackets of BTCUSDT and ETHUSDT are copied from Binance's public bracket data (updateTime 2025-08-19, retrieved 2026-10-25), except tier 1 at 125x instead of 150x (the system's ceiling, `@Max(125)` on pairs and brackets). Rejected: a staging-only SQL file (pairs and plans cannot be valid rows before the backend runs); a Java runner in the `demo` profile (would need a public plan API in `trading`).  (5) **Start-up guard** (the team lead's condition of approval, 2026-10-25): property `cryptopilot.environment` (`CRYPTOPILOT_ENVIRONMENT`), `development` in the base configuration and `production` under `prod`. `common.config.DemoEnvironmentGuard`, an `EnvironmentPostProcessor` registered in `META-INF/spring.factories` (not a bean, so it runs before Flyway), refuses to start when `demo` is active and the environment is not `development` or `staging` (a typo or an empty value is refused too). Staging therefore sets `CRYPTOPILOT_ENVIRONMENT=staging` beside `prod,demo`. `DemoEnvironmentGuardTest` covers both sides, including a real start on `prod,demo` that is refused. The guard checks the `demo` profile only; `dev` also lists `db/demo` and is not guarded. | APPROVED (team lead, 2026-10-25, with the guard of (5)) | DEMO-01, D-19, D-62, T-096 |
| D-121 | 2026-10-25 | **The capital of a trading plan is the Trader's account size (TP-D; amends BR-23 and UC-06 input).** (1) A plan's capital is the default capital of the Trader's profile; every plan of the Trader uses that one number, and the Trader no longer enters a capital per plan (create, update and calculate stop accepting it; responses keep it for display). (2) The risk % stays per plan, defaulted from the profile as today. (3) The plan stores the capital as a snapshot at every save and recalculates it at activation (BR-31); a later profile change affects only later saves and activations, never a stored snapshot; existing plans keep their stored capital (no backfill). (4) A profile with no default capital (null or ≤ 0) refuses the plan request with MSG01 on `capital`. (5) TOTAL_OPEN_RISK (BR-29) divides the summed risk of ACTIVE plans and open positions (D-119 (3)) by the CURRENT profile capital, not by each plan's stored capital. (6) BR-25 and BR-26 compare against the snapshot capital; activation re-checks them against the current profile capital. Breaking change for the web: the SCR-17 capital input becomes read-only and links to SCR-07 | APPROVED | Team lead, 2026-10-23 (TP-D) |
| D-122 | 2026-10-25 | **Exit fills of a simulated position (team lead decision of 2026-10-24; answers Q-61; T-048b).** (1) The take profit is a resting limit order and pays the maker fee (D-97), so it fills at the take profit price, never at a better candle open, also through a gap (as E7 for resting LIMIT entries): on a continuous limit order book a resting order trades at its own price; a take-profit-market order (taker) is the other coherent model and is not the one D-97 chose. (2) The stop loss stays a stop-market order: taker fee, fills at the candle open when the candle opened beyond it, then is moved by `SLIPPAGE_BPS` against the Trader (`SlippageCalculator.takerFill`). (3) Q-61: when the candle open is already at or past one level, that level decides (TP at the TP price, SL at the open); otherwise, when one range reaches both, the stop loss goes first (BR-35). (4) This differs on purpose from the paper module's TAKE_PROFIT (Q-51), a market order with a taker fee. `ExitRule.byRange` (T-044) changed accordingly; TD 7.7 assumption 2 | APPROVED | Team lead, 2026-10-24 (T-048b brief) |
| D-123 | 2026-10-25 | **T-048b engine and journal choices.** (1) Entry-candle rule (Q-59): inside the 1-minute candle in which the entry filled (the candle that opened at or before `entry_time` and ended after it) only the stop loss can act, by the candle's adverse extreme, from the entry price: a STOP gap-filled at the open can be stopped in that candle at its SL, and a LIMIT filled at activation already beyond its SL exits at once at its entry price (then slips); the entry candle never takes profit (same pessimism as D-112 R5). A candle that ended at or before `entry_time` never acts on the position. Liquidation in the entry candle is allowed. **Changed by the team lead (2026-10-25):** a plan whose stop loss the current last price has already reached at activation is refused with MSG16 on `stopLoss` for every entry type (LIMIT, STOP, MARKET), on Activate and on Save and Activate, Futures by the last price, never the mark price (extends the D-112 refusals; a MARKET is checked against its unslipped last price, before the deviation check); the immediate exit at the entry price stays only as an engine safety net for replay edge cases. (2) Exit time = open time of the deciding candle, last price or mark price (as D-78), so a live tick and a replayed mark candle give the same row. (3) The liquidation price of a position is computed at its fill price with the brackets at the fill and stored in `trading_journal.liquidation_price` (V36, backfilled from the plan's estimate); the plan's estimate is kept when it cannot be computed. (4) The close compare-and-sets on the journal row's status and version (D-119); `fee_amount` = stored `entry_fee_amount` + exit fee. (5) Incomplete funding is corrected by a scheduled journal job (every 10 min) that asks `trading.TradeFunding` (new root port of `trading`, implemented by `FundingServiceImpl`, since `journal` may only use `trading`'s public API, D-119 (1)); there is no event for a late settlement. (6) `PositionClosed` also carries planId, market, symbol and exitPrice (MSG23/MSG24 need them). (7) `/user/queue/positions` pushes on close only; open positions are read through the API. (8) Excursions are kept by the engine and carried on the exit; writing them is T-048c, because a restart loses them until T-048c stores them on closed candles. **Also required by the team lead:** a STOMP integration test of `/user/queue/positions` (owner receives, another user and a guest cannot subscribe) and a test where a gap fill moves the recomputed liquidation price past the stop loss and the mark price liquidates first | APPROVED with the changes above (team lead, 2026-10-25) | T-048b (BE2) |
| D-124 | 2026-10-23 | **Changing the levels of an open simulated position (team lead, 2026-10-23; TP-E).** (1) A Trader may change the stop loss and the take profit of an OPEN simulated position. (2) Partial closing stays out of scope (D-11). (3) Realized R keeps the ORIGINAL planned risk (BR-42), so the journal shows the effect of moving stops. (4) Storage per D-119 (5): the current levels live only on the journal row, versioned by its optimistic lock; `trading` changes them only through the `TradeJournal` port; the journal row keeps the original levels and a history table of changes. (5) Built in TP-E: TOTAL_OPEN_RISK (BR-29) counts an open position at its CURRENT risk, quantity × |entry − current SL| on the losing side and 0 at or past the entry, over the profile capital (amends D-119 (3), which summed the plan's `risk_amount`). (6) Change timing (Q-80, approved by the team lead 2026-10-26): a change acts on the candles that open at or after its instant, in practice from the next 1-minute candle; a candle that opened before it is decided by the levels in force at its open (as D-77), in live processing and in a replay alike, so replay equals live. The change response and the position block return `levelsEffectiveFrom`, the open time of that candle, for the web's "new levels apply from HH:mm". (7) A stop widened beyond the risk profile stays a non-blocking WARNING (`OVERSIZED_POSITION`, `TOTAL_OPEN_RISK`, the severities of plan creation); only a Futures stop at or past the liquidation price is BLOCKING (MSG16); `stop_widened` and R on the original risk record the habit (Q-79, team lead 2026-10-26). | APPROVED | Team lead 2026-10-23; A-52; TP-E |
**Action:** D-02 and D-03 were confirmed by the team on 2026-09-19 and are `APPROVED`; D-18 on 2026-09-20. D-04 … D-09 still need confirmation; D-06 and D-07 are already load-bearing in committed code (T-008). D-19 and D-20 are load-bearing as of T-007 and need confirmation before the first staging deployment, because D-20 decides how an operator obtains the first administrator. D-21 and D-22 are load-bearing as of T-010; D-21 is the one worth arguing about, because reversing it later means touching every entity that was written under it. D-29 and D-30 are load-bearing as of T-100; D-31 and D-32 as of T-013. D-23 … D-26 are load-bearing as of T-011 and T-012; D-26 is the one to re-read if the refresh windows of SRS 3.2.3 are ever changed, because it infers "Remember me" from the length of the window rather than from a stored flag.

SRS v1.0 BR-27 edited in place (Draft 1.0, no version bump); implemented by T-036 | T-036 |

See D-97. SRS BR-35, BR-36, BR-38, BR-42 edited in place | — |

| A-29 | Two endpoints return the same answer for every address, which SRS 3.2.2 and 3.2.4 require, but not in the same **time**. `requestPasswordReset`: an address that gets a link costs a bulk update, an insert and an event that an unknown or unverified one does not. `resendVerification` is **worse, with three tiers rather than two** - unknown or already verified returns after one query; known, unverified and throttled returns after the throttle check, which is two more queries; known, unverified and not throttled goes on to write and publish. So it leaks not only whether an address holds an unverified account but whether that address has asked recently. `login` in the same class equalises exactly this kind of difference with a bcrypt verification against a hash of nothing, so this is a standard the codebase already sets and neither of these paths meets | Decide the fix when the mail module lands: move the issuing work off the request thread so every case returns after the same lookup, which is the seam A-18 already has to open. **Not a constant delay** - that is a new number nobody chose, it costs every caller, and it leaks under statistics anyway. Found by self-review of T-013; the `resendVerification` half was found when an external review grouped the two endpoints together as solved, which they are at the level of the response body and are not at the level of the clock | SRS 3.2.2, 3.2.4, SCR-05, BR-01, BR-04 | PENDING; PROPOSED 2026-10-22: issue through the mail outbox off the request thread so every case returns after the same lookup, see `../reviews/OPEN_ITEMS_2026-10-22.md` | the task that adds the mail module |

| A-33 | T-020's "Done when" says "Gap detection works", and NSF-02 says the backfill runs "when a gap is detected". Read as: NSF-02 fills the tail gap (latest stored candle → now) on every run (T-020); NSF-03 detects every other gap (T-021) and hands it to NSF-02 | **Implemented in T-021 and extended by the team lead (2026-09-24):** (1) each closed streamed candle is compared with the latest stored one of its series; a later one raises `GapDetected`, queued in memory by `CandleBackfillJob` and filled with a bounded range; an empty series reports its whole D-41 depth; (2) a reconnection after a loss starts a catch-up at once; (3) **at start-up**, `OhlcvRepository.gapsSince` scans the last `gap-scan-window` (7 days) with `lag(open_time)` over each (pair, market, timeframe) and queues every hole, which replaces persisting the in-memory queue. An edit of such a post cannot drop any of its files (400 `MEDIA_KEPT_AS_EVIDENCE`, MSG56 on `media`, A-49). Retention: the kept files stay with no time limit, also for a post Admin restored after HIDE_POST and its author then deleted. Known limits: the author of a published post with an open report can still edit its text (3.7.2), not its media; a report and a delete of the same post running at the same moment are not serialised (no lock on the post), so the delete may release the files of a post reported in that instant (P3, rare) | SRS 3.7.2, 3.11.3, BR-47 | DONE (SRS edited, Record of Changes 22/10/2026) | 6068355, 59c4913, e08ecdb |

## 7. Detail block — current NEXT task

### T-039 · Trading plan API

**State 2026-10-01:** DONE on PR #41 (`feat/t-039-trading-plan-api`, BE1), awaiting review and merge; criteria 1–10 met, `clean verify` 1889/1889. T-038 merged (PR #40, `e7ce968`). Refs UC-16…UC-20, BR-21…BR-33, BR-62, BR-66, CR-04, CR-08, MSG15…MSG20, MSG27, MSG29; TD 4.3, 5.1, 5.5, 8; D-61, D-63, D-66…D-69.

**Phase 0 findings**
- **Endpoints (TD 8):** `POST /plans/calculate` (no persistence), `GET /plans`, `POST /plans`, `GET /plans/{id}`, `PUT /plans/{id}`, `POST /plans/{id}/activate`, `POST /plans/{id}/cancel`; `POST /plans/{id}/close-position` is UC-21 and waits for open positions (T-042). `/api/v1/plans/**` is already TRADER-only in `SecurityConfig` (D-27). TD 4.3 names `TradingPlanController`, `CreateTradingPlanRequest`, `TradingPlanResponse`, `TradingPlanService`.
- **Open positions:** `trading_journal` has no code (journal module, T-042/T-046), so open simulated positions count **0** today: the `ACTIVE_PLAN_MAX` count and the other open risk read ACTIVE plans only.
- **D-63 lock key:** `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext(user_id::text))`, taken before the count, in the transaction that activates. No advisory lock exists in the code yet.
- **Missing cross-module reads:** `market` exports only `PairFilters`, `SetupStyleSource` and its enums — no pair lookup, last price, funding rate or leverage brackets; `user` exports the risk profile (`RiskProfileApi`) but not the default capital / risk %; the three BR-29 thresholds the profile does not define (`WARN_LOW_RR_RATIO`, `WARN_WIDE_STOP_LOSS_PERCENT`, `WARN_HIGH_FUNDING_RATE`) are in `system_setting`, owned by `admin`, which has no code. Each is added as a read-only root API of its owning module.
- **Leverage brackets:** `leverage_bracket` is empty in every environment: nothing collects it (Binance's bracket endpoint is signed, i.e. needs an API key, which the product never uses) and its administration is T-085 (UC-46). A Futures plan therefore cannot be sized until an administrator enters brackets.
- **Last price:** the Redis cache (`PriceCacheService.latest`) says FOUND / EXPIRED / MISSING / UNAVAILABLE; a MARKET entry needs FOUND with a last price. SRS 5.3 has no message for "no current price".
- **Entitlements:** `EntitlementApi.requireFeature` (MSG29) and `requireWithinLimit` (MSG27 with max, items, tier); Futures plans are gated by `FUTURES_ANALYSIS` (the only Futures switch of D-58). No consumer exists yet (T-107 READY).
- `ObjectOptimisticLockingFailureException` is not mapped by `GlobalExceptionHandler` (a race answers 500 today).

**Acceptance criteria**
1. V16 widens `trading_plan.risk_reward_ratio` to `numeric(20,8)`; contract csv updated; a ratio ≥ 10000 persists.
2. `TradingPlanService` (service + impl, D-48) with one `@Transactional` method per use case and the injected `Clock`: `calculate`, `create` (optionally "Save and Activate"), `update` (optionally activate), `activate`, `cancel`, `get`, `list`.
3. Create/update: pair enabled on the market (else 404 MSG41); Futures needs `FUTURES_ANALYSIS` (MSG29); capital and risk % default from the profile (risk % falls back to the profile's risk per trade), a missing capital is MSG01 on `capital`; prices rounded to the tick (BR-30); MARKET entry = current last price (none current → 503); LIMIT expiry defaults to now + 7 days; `otherOpenRisk` = risk of the user's other ACTIVE plans (+ open positions, 0 today); calculation, liquidation, warnings; inputs the calculation rejects → 400 with `errors` field → MSG15/MSG16/MSG01.
4. Activate: D-63 lock first, then count ACTIVE plans (+ open positions), `requireWithinLimit(ACTIVE_PLAN_MAX)` → MSG27; recalculate with the current price and other open risk; `TradingPlan.activate` (MSG18 on a BLOCKING warning). Count and update in one transaction.
5. Get/update/activate/cancel load the plan by id **and** owner; another user's plan is 404, never 403.
6. List: tabs DRAFT / ACTIVE / EXECUTED / CANCELLED_EXPIRED, pair, market, created-at period; 20 per page by default (CR-04), newest first; one statement for the page + one count; warnings not loaded. Detail loads the warnings with one more statement.
7. Responses: inputs, snapshot, warnings with severity, status, status history from the instants (D-67).
8. `ObjectOptimisticLockingFailureException` → 409 `DATA_CONFLICT`.
9. Tests: every use case; FREE + Futures → MSG29; FREE 3 ACTIVE → 4th MSG27; PRO unlimited; BLOCKING → MSG18; MARKET past SL → 400 `errors.stopLoss` MSG16; other user → 404; TOTAL_OPEN_RISK uses the other plans; concurrency 2 ACTIVE + 2 DRAFT, two parallel activations → exactly one MSG27, 20 repetitions; web: statuses, problem shape, paging, 401, 403 for ADMIN; list query count.
10. Touched classes 100 % line / ≥ 95 % branch; global ≥ 99.61 % / 95.98 %.

**Out of scope:** fills, BR-33 immediate fill (a MARKET plan stays ACTIVE until T-042), journal, fees, funding, NSF-09, notifications, close position (T-042); FE (T-040/T-041); stored warning severity (follow-up).

| Q-21 | T-029 is "Analysis API **and STOMP push**", and NSF-05 says the result "is pushed to clients viewing the pair", but TD 9 defines no destination for it: its public topics are ticker, kline and overview, and the analysis is Trader-only (SRS 3.1.3; `/api/v1/analysis/**` is TRADER). Three things are undecided. (1) **Destination**: a topic per series, e.g. `/topic/analysis.{market}.{symbol}.{tf}`, admitted only to an authenticated TRADER session, or a per-user `/user/queue/analysis` fed from what each session views. (2) **Payload**: the setup score depends on the caller's preset and is read on the preset's timeframe, so a series topic can carry the preset-independent part (indicators, S/R, components of `tf`) with the client re-reading the score by REST, or also the score of every preset whose timeframe is `tf`. (3) **CONNECT authentication**: TD 9 describes the bearer on CONNECT, but `WebSocketConfig` admits only the public topics and D-51 left the bearer check to the task that adds a non-public destination — this one would be it | 2026-09-27 | T-029 (push half), T-032, T-033, T-034 (live update of the analysis) | Team lead | **OPEN** — recommended: (1) the series topic, TRADER-only, with the bearer check of TD 9 on CONNECT; (2) the preset-independent part only, plus `openTime`, and the client re-reads `GET /analysis` for the score — one message per candle close, no per-user fan-out, and the score logic stays in one place. Not built until answered: a public topic would leak Trader-only data, and a guessed per-user design is hard to undo; PROPOSED 2026-10-22: take the recommendation in this row (TRADER-only series topic, preset-independent payload, score re-read by REST), see `../reviews/OPEN_ITEMS_2026-10-22.md` |

| Q-37 | Kiến trúc engine paper cho hàng chục nghìn lệnh cùng lúc. User đã chọn 2026-10-10: GIỮ Postgres làm nguồn sự thật duy nhất, BỎ Kafka (dự án là monolith, Kafka thêm hạ tầng và khoảng 1–4 GB RAM mà chưa service nào khác cần đọc luồng sự kiện). Hướng RAM + event log (LMAX/event sourcing, các bảng `paper_*` thành projection) tạm gác; nếu sau này mở lại thì gọi là "event log" để không nhầm với `TRADING_JOURNAL` của người dùng, và đặt trong bảng Postgres chỉ ghi thêm (group commit), không dùng Kafka. Số đo hiện tại trên laptop (CPU dùng chung 99%): burst 2000 lệnh MARKET không lỗi, 3000 thì 30% lỗi quá hạn pool 10 s; ổn định khoảng 453 lệnh/giây sau khi gom batch. Còn lại: đo lại `PaperOrderLoadTest` với Postgres tách khỏi bộ sinh tải; chỉ mở lại hướng RAM + event log nếu trần đo được thấp hơn nhu cầu. Two openings at the same moment (two Admins, or a client retry) race for the optimistic lock of `post_report` and the second gets 409. Make the claim tolerate a concurrent opening (update only OPEN rows in one statement, or retry once), or document that a 409 means "opened elsewhere, read again" | 2026-10-23 | none (the web sends one opening) | BE (community) | OPEN |
| Q-77 | D-112 reads a plan's setup score on its `analysis_timeframe` under the Trader's preset, but a preset's weights are defined for its own timeframe only (SCALPING 15m, DAY_TRADING 1h, SWING 4h; TD 7.4) and `SetupScoreCalculator` refuses components of another timeframe. When the two differ (the Trader picked another timeframe, any plan on 1d, or the style changed after the draft save), which score is stored? Options: (a) null (built in TP-A: the read returns a null score, nothing blocks); (b) the preset's score on its own timeframe, as the analysis screen shows (then the stored score is not on `analysis_timeframe`); (c) the preset's weights applied to the components of `analysis_timeframe` (changes the T-029 rule; needs an SRS alignment item). Proposal: (a) until decided, since a null score never blocks and T-048 then makes no comparison | 2026-10-23 | T-048 (score at fill, MSG55); TP-A stores null in the mismatch case | Team lead | ANSWERED 2026-10-23 (team lead, final; replaces the first answer of the same day, which weighted the preset over the components of `analysis_timeframe`): (b). A plan stores the score the Setup Score card of the analysis screen shows at activation: the latest closed candle of the preset's own timeframe under the Trader's preset, whatever `analysis_timeframe` is (D-53). `analysis_timeframe` stays for the chart and for `fill_indicators` at the fill (T-048c); T-048c rescores with the stored preset and version on that preset's own timeframe. The candle open time stays derivable from `activated_at` and the preset's timeframe. Recorded as the D-112 note |
| Q-78 | UC-52 (SCR-44, view audit log: filters actor, action, entity type, period; read-only) has no task row, and SRS 3.11.9 keeps the log for one year while no job removes older rows (NSF-17 lists snapshots, tokens and notifications only). Who builds the read API, and should the retention be a cleanup job or a partition policy? | 2026-10-24 | UC-52, NSF-17 | Team lead | OPEN |
| Q-79 | TP-E (D-124): a stop loss moved further from the entry can break the risk profile (the position's risk above the risk per trade, or the total open risk above the maximum). TP-E stores the change and answers non-blocking `OVERSIZED_POSITION` / `TOTAL_OPEN_RISK` warnings in `position.levelWarnings`. Should such a change be refused instead (and with which message), or need a confirmation like MSG51? | 2026-10-25 | — | Team lead | **ANSWERED 2026-10-26 (team lead):** keep it a non-blocking WARNING, the severity of OVERSIZED_POSITION and TOTAL_OPEN_RISK at plan creation; only an SL beyond the liquidation price is BLOCKING; `stop_widened` and R on the original risk record the habit (D-124 (7)). Built as answered in TP-E |
| Q-80 | TP-E (D-124): the new levels act from the next 1-minute candle; the rest of the minute in which the change was made, and every older candle a replay serves, is decided by the levels in force at the candle's open. So replay always equals live, but a position can still exit at the earlier stop in that minute, or at an older candle replayed after a change committed during the replay (`PositionCloser` closes an exit decided at an older version when every change since was made after the exit's candle opened). The alternative (new levels at once for the rest of the minute) is friendlier but makes a replay of that minute differ from live. Confirm the chosen rule, or choose the alternative. | 2026-10-25 | — | Team lead | **ANSWERED 2026-10-26 (team lead):** approved, new levels apply from the next 1-minute candle so replay equals live (as D-77), on condition that the change response and the position block return `levelsEffectiveFrom` (open time of that candle), documented in the fe-guide; built in TP-E `996908d` with tests (D-124 (6)) |

Rule: an AI agent never answers an open question by guessing. It implements what is unambiguous, adds or updates the row here and marks the task `PARTIAL` or `BLOCKED`.

### 2026-10-26 – TP-E Change the stop loss and take profit of an open simulated position
- Result: DONE
- Summary: `PATCH /api/v1/plans/{id}/position-levels` (new `PositionLevelController`, `PositionLevelService`) changes the SL and/or TP of the caller's OPEN position: tick rounding, checks against the current last price (never mark), Futures SL before the stored liquidation price (MSG16 on the field), 404 / 409 MSG43 / 503 MSG43, under the D-63 lock, compare-and-set on the journal version through new `TradeJournal` methods (journal side: `changeLevelsIfOpen`, `LevelChangeLog`, V37 original levels, `stop_widened`, `level_change_count`, `trading_journal_level_change`). The engine applies a change from its instant: older candles use the levels in force at their open (history kept in the engine, rebuilt from the journal at start), so replay equals live; `PositionCloser` keeps an exit decided at an older version when every change since came after its candle opened (Q-80). TOTAL_OPEN_RISK counts open positions at their current stop; a widened stop warns (`OVERSIZED_POSITION` / `TOTAL_OPEN_RISK`, Q-79). Position block: current/original levels, history, `breakevenPrice` (tick-grid search through FeeSchedule, SlippageCalculator, TradeResultCalculator, TradeAmounts) and `breakevenProvisional`.

### 2026-10-24 – T-045 Fees, funding settlement and realized P/L (follow-up: incomplete funding, gap safety net)
- Result: DONE
- Summary: D-97 note recorded (team lead): an incomplete funding never blocks a close. `FundingFee(amount, incomplete, missingFundingTimes)` always carries the known sum; `TradeResultCalculator.calculate(TradeClose, FundingFee)` takes it for Futures (Spot still null, funding refused); `TradeResult`/`TradeAmounts` expose the flag and `netPnl()` returns `NetPnl(amount, provisional)`. Safety net in `FundingCalculator`: a snapshot gap overlapping the period (inner, leading or trailing edge) longer than the funding interval expects the pair's funding times in it and names those not stored; interval = shortest distance between consecutive announced times with no gap between their snapshots, else between stored settlements, else none (a gap then makes it incomplete with no names); a gap of at most one interval hides nothing (team lead agreed, also for edges). `FuturesMetricsRepository.fundingHistory` aggregates in SQL (announcements grouped with first/last snapshot, gaps by `lag()` with the window ends as sentinels, look-back day settlements) instead of reading every snapshot. `settleFunding` logs a WARN per settlement without mark price. Replay (Q-34): goes through the backfill weight share and `BinanceRequestGate`, no change.
- Files: `market/{FundingAnnouncement,SnapshotGap,FundingHistory,MarketApi}.java`, `market/repository/FuturesMetricsRepository.java`, `market/service/impl/{MarketApiImpl,FuturesMetricsServiceImpl}.java`, `trading/calculator/{FundingCalculator,TradeResultCalculator}.java`, `trading/model/{FundingFee,NetPnl,TradeAmounts,TradeResult}.java`, `trading/service/{FundingService,impl/FundingServiceImpl}.java`; TD 7.8 (decision, safety net, worked example, known limitation of an interval change inside a gap, accepted as rare); D-97 note; T-048 part b
- Tests: +25 (FundingCalculatorTest +14 incl. review scenario, both rows stored, short gap, 4h symbol, edges; FundingHistoryQueryTest +4 against TimescaleDB; TradeResultCalculatorTest +3; TradeAmountsTest +2; FundingServiceImplTest +1; FuturesMetricsServiceTest +1); suite 3348/3348 (`clean verify`); coverage Domain (`calculator` packages) 100% line / Backend 97.3% line; gates hold
- BR/UC/NSF covered: BR-11, BR-37, BR-38, NSF-04, NSF-08 (A-05), D-97 rule 3 note
- Decisions: D-97 note 2026-10-24 (team lead); edge gaps follow the same length rule as inner gaps (team lead, 2026-10-24); interval change inside a gap accepted as a known limitation
- Open questions: none
- Next: TP-0 (T-048b takes the journal columns)

### 2026-10-24 – T-045 Fees, funding settlement and realized P/L
- Result: DONE
- Summary: A-05 (option 1) and Q-34 recorded as decided by the team lead (2026-10-23). `MarketApi#fundingHistory` (added at the end of the interface) reads the settlements of `funding_rate_history` in (after, upTo] and the funding times the mark price snapshots announced (`next_funding_time`), so the pair's funding times are known without assuming an interval. `FundingCalculator` sums `−d × qty × mark × rate` over entry < t ≤ exit at full precision, rounds once (HALF_EVEN, scale 8); a missing announced settlement gives an incomplete result with no amount and a WARN naming the pair and the instant; `FundingService` recomputes on every call, so a late row is picked up by the next read. `TradeResult` carries `fundingFeeAmount` (null for Spot) and `netPnl()` delegates to `TradeAmounts.netPnl`, the only Java net formula (grep: no other place). `replay.max-window` default 24h → 7d.
- Files: `market/{FundingHistory,SettledFunding,MarketApi}.java`, `market/repository/FuturesMetricsRepository.java`, `market/service/impl/MarketApiImpl.java`, `trading/calculator/{FundingCalculator,TradeResultCalculator}.java`, `trading/model/{FundingFee,TradeAmounts,TradeResult}.java`, `trading/service/FundingService.java`, `trading/service/impl/FundingServiceImpl.java`, `trading/config/MatchingProperties.java`; TD 7.7, 7.8; traceability BR-37, BR-38, NSF-08

- D-111 recorded (APPROVED by the team lead 2026-10-22), with A-48 and the GY tracking table (section 4b). D-84, A-41 and D-97 rule 2 carry amendment notes. BR/MSG/settings added:
  - SRS edited in place (1.0 Draft kept): BR-32 (INVALIDATED), BR-33 (STOP, gap fill, Futures last price), BR-36 (fees by liquidity role, slippage), NSF-09 (table and 3.5.4), 3.5.1 fields, 3.5.2 tab, 3.5.3 activation, 3.11.8 settings, 5.4.1 assumption 4;
  - new BR-68 (invalidation), BR-69 (entry deviation), BR-70 (learning data);
  - new MSG51 (deviation confirm), MSG52 (deviation refused), MSG53 (expired), MSG54 (invalidated), MSG55 (setup score drop). MSG50 is skipped because of Q-66;
  - settings ENTRY_DEVIATION_CONFIRM_R 0.25, ENTRY_DEVIATION_BLOCK_R 0.75, SETUP_SCORE_DROP_WARN 15, SLIPPAGE_BPS 0;
  - TD 7.7 (last price per market, STOP book, invalidation table and order, excursions, simulation assumptions) and 7.8 (liquidity role, slippage, deviation, R-multiple, expectancy, MAE/MFE with LONG and SHORT worked examples), references; logical model.
- Verification: E1..E7 confirmed/disproved:
  - E1 CONFIRMED: `TradeResultCalculator:29` picks maker or taker by entry type, so a marketable LIMIT pays maker.
  - E2 CONFIRMED: the futures `kline_1m` is subscribed and its close is the last trade, but `MarketStreamSupervisor:233-235` returns before the cache and `PriceCacheServiceImpl:119-131` leaves the Futures `lastPrice` null.
  - E3 CONFIRMED: a Futures LIMIT never fills at activation (`TradingPlanServiceImpl:348-354`). It fills a minute later at the entry price, as maker.
  - E4 CONFIRMED: only status, `executed_at` and `fill_price` are stored (`TradingPlan:254-261`). There is no journal code yet. Activation overwrites a MARKET plan's draft price (`TradingPlan:239`).
  - E5 CONFIRMED absent: there is no slippage.
  - E6 CONFIRMED: `MinuteKlineFeed:417-421` drops the candle open and close. The assumptions were not written down; they are now in TD 7.7.
  - E7 CONFIRMED and kept: a resting LIMIT fills at its own price through a gap (`EntryFillRule:61-63`).
  - Side finding: `openPositions` returns 0, so an EXECUTED plan frees its `ACTIVE_PLAN_MAX` slot today. This is noted on T-048.
- Tasks created: TP-A, TP-0, TP-B, TP-C (BE1, new section P6c), T-113 (FE). Scope added to T-046, T-048, T-051, T-074 and FE (T-040, T-041, T-052). Header "Next task": BE1 T-045 → TP-A → TP-0 → TP-B → TP-C → T-107; BE2 T-046 → T-048 (needs TP-A).

### 2026-10-14 – Spot order types aligned with the exchange's Spot API (TR-09…TR-12 rework)
- Result: DONE; `feat/tr-02-paper-trading-schema` @ `e05dd5e`, pushed
- Summary: review against the Binance Spot API docs found the Spot stops named after Futures orders (STOP_LIMIT, TRAILING_STOP_LIMIT with callbackRate %, GTX post-only). Spot now takes STOP_LOSS, STOP_LOSS_LIMIT, TAKE_PROFIT, TAKE_PROFIT_LIMIT with stopPrice and/or trailingDelta (BIPS 10–2000); trigger side fixed by type and side, a stop already met refused (PAPER_ORDER_WOULD_TRIGGER); trailing tracks peak/trough from the stop condition or the next trade (Trailing Stop FAQ); stop-market executes at the book on trigger; GTX refused on Spot (LIMIT_MAKER is post-only); MARKET SELL by quoteOrderQty; OCO TP leg LIMIT_MAKER, SL leg STOP_LOSS_LIMIT or STOP_LOSS; OTO pending any Spot stop. Futures types renamed to the Futures API (STOP, STOP_MARKET, TAKE_PROFIT, TAKE_PROFIT_MARKET, TRAILING_STOP_MARKET).
- Files: V24 migration (renames stored Spot stops, rewrites order checks); `OrderType`, `TriggerOrder`, `TriggerBook`, `PaperOrder`, `SpotArrival`, `PaperOrderLists`, `PaperOrderServiceImpl`, `PaperMatchingServiceImpl`, DTOs
- Tests: suite 2750/2750 (`./mvnw clean verify`, coverage checks met); TriggerBookTest rewritten for the exchange's trailing rules; new API tests for stop-market, MARKET sell by quote, OCO with STOP_LOSS, OCO take profit that would take, OTO pending stop, group field rules
- Decisions: none new (follows D-92: "like Binance")

### 2026-10-14 – Realtime review against current Binance and TradingView docs; six fixes (cryptopilot-frontend)
- Result: six fixes on `fix/realtime-hardening` (local, not pushed, not merged)
- Summary: the owner asked whether the web handles crypto realtime correctly rather than only "running". Checked against the current Binance docs and changelogs and TradingView's: the backend already meets the 2025–2026 changes (Futures streams on the new `/market` route, legacy URLs decommissioned 2026-04-23; `<symbol>@ticker`, not the retired `!ticker@arr`; renewal after 23 h with overlap, pong by the JDK client, back-off with jitter, idle watchdog, decimals as `BigDecimal`). Two ideas from an older FE note do not fit this project: Binance SBE streams need an Ed25519 API key (the system never uses exchange keys) and TradingView Advanced Charts is licensed only to companies for public web projects, not to studies, so Lightweight Charts (D-103) stays. Fixed in the web: (1) `useFrameState` queued every depth and trade update until the next animation frame, and a hidden tab runs none, so the queue grew without bound; updates are now folded as they arrive (a test fails on the old code). (2) The charts hid the TradingView logo and credited the library nowhere; its licence asks for the NOTICE and a link to tradingview.com, now in the shell footer. (3) A half-open socket (no WebSocket ping visible to scripts, no STOMP heart-beats from the broker) left the screens Delayed for good; 30 s of silence on a steady topic now reconnects, the limit doubling to 5 min, the socket discarded at once, heart-beats asked for both ways. (4) The trades list is read again whenever the feed opens (subscribe first, then read). (5) The forming candle rebuilt the candle list, recomputed every indicator over 1,000 candles and rewrote every series each push; it now updates one bar with `series.update()`. (6) Charts showed UTC; intraday charts now show local time with one fixed offset and name the zone (D-110)
- Tests: web suite 364/364 (format, types, lint, build green; tests also run under TZ=UTC and America/New_York for the chart time); no browser check this time (the machine had 0.5 GB of free memory and the background servers had been stopped for it)
- Decisions: D-110 (PROPOSED)
- Open questions: none new
- Next: (1) Owner: approve or change D-110; review and merge `fix/realtime-hardening` (and the four branches of 2026-10-13: `feature/TR-F3-post-only`, `fix/TR-F2-book-groups`, `feature/T-059-funding-alerts`, `feature/T-016-session-restore`) into `develop`; push when asked. (2) BE suggestions, not FE blockers: treat Binance `serverShutdown` (sent 10 minutes before a disconnect) as an early overlapped renewal; enable STOMP heart-beats on the broker so the web's heart-beat settings take effect. (3) Browser check of the six fixes once memory allows (hidden tab for 10 minutes on the trading screen; footer credit; "Times in UTC+7")
- Commits: `fix/realtime-hardening` `574f5d1`, `288a309`, `985a1c7`, `3d66fa4`, `1282f09`, `cfbbbfc` (local)

### 2026-10-13 – TR-F5 Spot part: demo orders and fills on the trading chart (cryptopilot-frontend)
- Result: PARTIAL (TR-F5): the Spot part merged into `develop` of `cryptopilot-frontend` and pushed (`0564ceb`); the rest waits for TR-25, TR-28 and TR-F4
- Summary: the owner chose the Spot part of TR-F5 (no Flutter for now). The shared chart (`features/chart`) gained three neutral options: a price line may carry an action, shown with the line's label as a chip at the pane's left edge that follows the price while the chart pans and zooms; markers placed on the bar whose period contains their time; caller toggle pills beside the indicators. The Spot trading screen uses them: one dashed line per working LIMIT order of the pair (green buy, red sell) with a chip "Buy 0.00200 ✕" that cancels it (refusal as a toast), the latest page of fills as arrows (up buy, down sell), and an "Orders" toggle remembered on the device. A first layout with the button beside the axis covered the line title and the latest bars, so the chip moved to the left edge. No Figma frame: local ui-spec written, needs designer review. Without the pair id (FEQ-01) every pair is read and narrowed by symbol; data is read every 3 s until TR-08
- Files (web repository): `src/features/chart/components/{AnalysisChart.tsx,chart-markers.ts}`, `src/features/paper-trading/{model/chart-orders.ts,hooks/use-chart-orders.ts,chart-orders.test.tsx}`, `src/pages/trader/TradeSpotPage.tsx`

- Result: DONE (TR-05); 2 commits on `feat/tr-02-paper-trading-schema`, pushed (tip `af76d64`); working tree clean

- `af76d64` `feat(paper): fill waiting orders in queue order at their price`: `LimitFillMode.QUEUE` (default); `PriceRange` carries `soldAt`/`boughtAt` volume by price (value-keyed, 80000 = 80000.00); `PaperOrderBook` keeps the quantity ahead of each order (from `MarketApi.currentDepth` when the order rests, else read when a trade first reaches its price); `application.yml` `cryptopilot.paper.matching.limit-fill-mode: QUEUE`, `trade-silence: 5s`.

- Result: PARTIAL (TR-06 still PARTIAL); 1 commit on `feat/tr-02-paper-trading-schema`, pushed (tip `0b12a0c`); working tree clean
- Summary: `POST /api/v1/paper/orders` has its own rate-limit rule `paper-order`, 50 new orders per account in a 10 s window (Binance Spot), counted instead of the per-minute `api` rule; cancels and reads stay under `api`. 429 `RATE_LIMITED` (MSG50) with `Retry-After` as before. Dev 500, prod 50, env `RATE_LIMIT_PAPER_ORDERS`. Before that: the `Co-Authored-By` trailers of the three TR-02 commits were removed with `git filter-branch` (tree unchanged, verified by diff) and the branch pushed (`c2e0685..b75ceef`, plain push); the `commit-msg` and `pre-commit` hooks were installed in the project clone's `.git/hooks` (they were missing, which is how the trailers got in); the local backup branch of the old commits was deleted after the push and was never pushed.
- Files: `common/config/RateLimitProperties` (`paperOrders`, `paperOrdersWindow`), `common/web/RateLimitFilter` (second `FixedWindowRateLimiter` for the rule's own window), `common/config/WebEdgeConfig`, `application.yml`, `application-dev.yml`, `application-prod.yml`
- Tests: suite 2530/2530 + Spotless + JaCoCo (`./mvnw clean verify`, BUILD SUCCESS); new in RateLimitFilterTest: own limit and own window, per account; ProfileEdgeDefaultsTest and TrustedProxyStartupCheckTest updated

- Decisions: Q-37 (user, 2026-10-10): keep Postgres as the only source of truth of the paper engine; Kafka dropped (monolith, no second reader, 1–4 GB RAM); the RAM + event log design is shelved and, if reopened, uses an append-only Postgres table with group commit, named "event log" so it is not confused with `TRADING_JOURNAL`. Rate limit: placing an order is not an `api` call (Binance counts orders apart from request weight); fixed window kept (allows up to 2× at a window edge, accepted for paper).
- Discussed, not done: per-pair matching (one queue and thread per pair, or `pairId` hashed into N queues). Today one `paper-matching` thread serves every `BookKey`; fills are already stored in parallel per Trader (`fill-parallelism` 8). Worth it only if the load test shows the matching thread, not Postgres, is the bottleneck; if done, re-split `fill-parallelism` so the total stays under the connection pool. Sizing talk: 8 GB RAM is enough for the current design (backend 2 GB, Postgres 2.5–3 GB); about 10 000 online users needs ~16 GB without Kafka.
- Q-37 review (2026-10-10, end of session; NOT applied, waiting on the user): (1) the measurements do not cover what Q-37 asks: `PaperOrderLoadTest` only places Spot MARKET orders (one account per Trader, one pair, buy then sell), so it never goes through `PaperMatchingWorker`; 453 orders/s is the ceiling of the placement API, not of the engine. The riskiest case, N resting LIMITs at one price filled by one candle (`fill-parallelism` 8), is unmeasured; (2) p95 is printed by the test but not recorded; only max 9.3 s and single-order 24 ms are, against a p95 < 300 ms target; (3) the scenario is the easy case (no Trader lock or balance-row contention); (4) since `0b12a0c` one account places at most 50 orders/10 s, while the load test runs without rate limits, so say which one is measured; (5) no closing criterion: point it at TR-32, and settle the fill target, < 500 ms in `paper-trading-plan.md` 12.2 vs < 1 s in TR-32; (6) move the decision into a new row D-90 (next free id) and shorten Q-37 to "ANSWERED in part by D-90"; (7) Owner says User + Team while D-15 and Q-36 were approved by the team lead: D-90 is APPROVED only if the user is the team lead, else PROPOSED; (8) Blocks should be TR-32 only (TR-05, TR-06, TR-08 are no longer blocked); (9) the dangling "(b)"/"(c)" and the "1–4 GB" Kafka figure (about 1–1.5 GB is enough for small loads; the real reason is extra infrastructure in a monolith) should go; Q-37/Q-38 are in Vietnamese while the other questions are in English. ### 2026-10-09 – TR-05/TR-06/TR-07 Spot paper orders, matching, load test
- Result: PARTIAL (TR-07 DONE; TR-05, TR-06, TR-12, TR-32 PARTIAL); 5 commits on `feat/tr-02-paper-trading-schema`, not pushed; working tree clean
- Summary: Spot MARKET (quantity or quoteOrderQty) and LIMIT (GTC/IOC/FOK/GTX) orders, cancel, open orders, order and fill history; 0.1% maker/taker fee in the coin received; `PaperMatchingWorker` fills resting LIMITs from `kline_1m` ranges in price-time priority, stores fills of different accounts in parallel (`fill-parallelism`), retries, and replays missed candles from a watermark. Load test on the laptop: burst of 2000 simultaneous MARKET orders with 0 errors (max 9.3 s), 3000 → 30% fail on the 10 s pool timeout; sustained ceiling ~367 orders/s, ~453 after write batching. ## Update 2026-10-03 (re-review T-043)
- Re-review of PR #47 (head `569664b`, diff from `97095a6`) and PR #48 (head `bb226e0`, fix commits on top of `f5a5e08`). Each head and each old head has its own worktree. Read only: nothing edited, committed, pushed or approved on GitHub; probes and mutations were reverted.
- Verdicts: **#47 APPROVE WITH NITS**; **#48 APPROVE WITH NITS** (one residual R1 path, follow-up suggested).
- `./mvnw clean verify`: both EXIT 0. Reports: #47 2224 tests, #48 2272 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors (the author reported 2226 / 2274; gap not explained). ModularityTest, PackageConventionTest and LayerRules are green, so Q3 holds.
- Probes (copies in the session scratchpad):
  - R1: a stream drop, and a closed update that never came, each fail on `3f3c9de` and pass on `bb226e0`.
  - R2: after a give-up the watermark does not pass the lost candle; fails on `3f3c9de`, passes on `bb226e0`.
  - R5: with the overlap drop removed from `MinuteKlineFeed`, `MatchingReplayTest` now fails 3 of 7 ("watermark advances per minute"); before, it passed.
- R3 OK: the wait starts at 1 s, doubles to 30 s and loses up to 20 % to jitter; the deadline is 10 min from the first failure. The pending fill keeps its price and time, and a success after a retry fills once (the retry leaves the list before it runs, plus compare-and-set). A retry only runs on the pair's next range.
- R2 + Q-34: the watermark stays held until a restart. If the hold outlasts replay.max-window (24 h), the restart skips the candle that reached the plan, with a WARN. The plan is put back into the books from the ACTIVE LIMIT rebuild and fills later, at another price time, or expires. The ERROR names the hold but does not say a restart is required, nor that it must come within the window.
- V18, tested on timescale pg16 at V17 with seeded rows: EXECUTED LIMIT and MARKET rows get fill_price = entry_price. An EXECUTED MARKET row with a NULL entry_price fails `ck_trading_plan_fill_price`, so the migration aborts. Develop never writes such a row: MARKET stores the last price, and only engine-tracked LIMIT plans reach EXECUTED.
- Migration numbers: only `feat/t-043-entry-fill-rules` (V18) and `feat/t-043-kline-replay` (V18, V19) use V18/V19.
- Findings:
  - F1 (#48, Medium-Low) `MinuteKlineFeed:267-274`: when a runtime replay throws (for example a DB error in `pairs.findById`), `goLiveAfterReplay(cursor, true)` accepts the hole. The next closed candle then moves the watermark past minutes that were never replayed, which the probe confirms. An empty REJECTED/MALFORMED page has the same effect after the catch-ups. Suggest re-queueing the replay with a back-off on an exception, and keeping "accept the hole" for a confirmed empty exchange answer. Follow-up is fine.
  - F2 (#48, Nit) `MatchingWorker` give-up ERROR: say that a restart is required within replay.max-window.
  - F3 (#47, Nit) V18: document the precondition that no EXECUTED row has a NULL entry_price, or check it first and abort with a clear message.
  - F4 (#47, Nit) retries run only when a range arrives; fine with `kline_1m` (about every 2 s), worth a line in the Javadoc.
- Merge checklist: (1) merge #47 into develop; (2) retarget #48 to develop, then rebase and run `clean verify` (V19 after V18); (3) merge #48; (4) open a follow-up for F1 (or fix it in #48); (5) Q-34 stays OPEN; (6) close issues by hand after each merge.

## Update 2026-10-03 (review T-043)
- Independent review of PR #47 (head `97095a6`) and PR #48 (head `3f3c9de`, stacked on #47), one worktree each. Read only: nothing edited, committed, pushed or approved on GitHub.
- Verdicts: **#47 APPROVE WITH NITS**; **#48 CHANGES REQUESTED** (R1, R2).
- `./mvnw clean verify`: #47 2220/2220, #48 2256/2256, coverage gates met on both; `ModularityTest`, `PackageConventionTest`, `LayerRulesTest` green; no flaky test seen in a single run of each.
- PR #47 against the specs: LIMIT LONG/SHORT with inclusive bounds DONE (`EntryFillRule:42`); a gap through the entry fills at the entry price, which is what BR-33 says (`EntryFillRule:61`); MARKET and a LIMIT already reached fill at activation at the last price, `executed_at` = activation instant, as D-78 allows (`TradingPlanServiceImpl:327-349`); D-77 `from < activated_at`, with a candle that opens exactly at activation allowed to fill and tested at ±1 ms (`EntryFillRule:52`); D-78 holds on the live, retry and replay paths (`Fill` is built once, and `PendingFill` keeps it unchanged); BR-34 sets EXECUTED only, with the journal record left to T-048 (not counted against the PR). N1: one segment per pair per candle plus one per command, drained after every command, so growth is at most one segment per pair per minute of backlog, with no hard cap. N3 and N4 DONE as asked.
- PR #48: `market` does not import `trading` (port `market.MinuteKlineListener`); replay is off by default and switched on explicitly by its tests; no test touches the network (MarketApi `@MockitoBean`, local `StubExchange` HttpServer). V18 follows the `binance_ban` pattern (pk_/ck_, new table only, no data touched; rollback = drop the table and the V18 history row, after which a replay starts from the first activation minute, capped by the window). Min streams 5→6 is a validation floor only; the configured 100 gives 16 pairs per connection instead of 20 (more shards, re-pack on deploy, no other effect).
- Mutation check (scratch worktree, removed afterwards): with the overlap drop removed from `MinuteKlineFeed` (`headMap(...).clear()` and the late-minute drop), `MinuteKlineFeedTest` fails 1 of 18, but **`MatchingReplayTest` still passes**: the acceptance test feeds no live update after the restart. Two uncommitted probes pass on the PR head and confirm R1 and R2; the copy is in the session scratchpad.
- Findings:
  - R1 (#48, High) `MatchingWorker:439` + `MinuteKlineFeed:333-340`: the watermark moves to any closed candle, even one that does not follow the previous watermark. A stream outage while the app runs (network loss or an idle-timeout abort; the planned 23 h renewal overlaps its connections and loses nothing) is logged, and then the first closed candle after the reconnect moves the watermark past the hole, so a restart never replays it either. A missing `x=true` for minute M is not detected at all (M's forming updates count as seen), and the watermark jumps M-1 → M+1. Lost: every candle in the hole and the final seconds of the minute in progress at the drop. Entries reached there fill later at another `executed_at`, or never (expiry), and replay no longer equals live. Fix that reuses the replay path: on a hole in `PairFeed.hand` (`from > lastClosed + 1 min`, tracked from closed ranges, not `lastOpen`), switch the pair to REPLAYING with `replayFrom(lastClosed + 1 min)` and queue a `Replay` on a long-lived replayer thread (the same `replay()` and `goLiveAfterReplay` code). Until the hole is replayed, hand the ranges on with `closed=false` (or make the advance contiguous) so that a crash in between still replays it.
  - R2 (#48, Medium) `MatchingWorker:439`: after `max-fill-attempts` the fill leaves `retries` and the next closed candle advances the watermark past it (probe). The plan stays ACTIVE, but the candle that reached it is never replayed, which contradicts "a restart replays that candle". Fix: on give-up keep the pair's watermark frozen (per-pair flag in the partition), or put the entry back in the books.
  - R3 (#47, Medium) `MatchingWorker:440-443, 461-476`: the retry budget is counted in ranges, not time. At 1–4 `kline_1m` updates per second, 5 attempts last about 1–5 s, so a short database blip uses them up. The entry is already out of the books, so the plan cannot fill again before a restart. N3 is met as written. Suggest a time-based backoff with a deadline (e.g. retry on closed candles only, or exponential up to a few minutes).
  - R4 (#48, Low) `MinuteKlineFeed:296-297`: with nothing held (stream not connected yet), `goLiveAfterReplay` skips the hole check, so a candle that just closed and is not yet served by REST is never fetched. Also require `cursor >= minuteOf(now)` before going live.
  - R5 (#48, Low) `MatchingReplayTest`: make it fail on a double-applied minute. Feed live updates of minutes 5–9 during and after the replay (including a late closed one) and assert that `fill(...)` is called once per plan.
  - R6 (#48, Low) `MinuteKlineServiceImpl:55`: `@Transactional(readOnly)` holds a DB connection for the duration of the REST call; the backfill does not do this. Keep the transaction to the pair lookup.
  - R7 (#47, Nit) `TradingPlanServiceImpl:107`: `activatedAt` keeps sub-microsecond precision in the event, but `timestamptz` stores microseconds, so in a window of under 1 µs after a minute open D-77 decides differently live and after a restart. Truncate `now` to micros.
  - R8 (#48, Nit) `MinuteKlineFeed:170-172`: with a watermark, the replay ignores the first activation, so an old watermark replays up to 24 h and logs a WARN about minutes that had no entry. Use `max(watermark + 1 min, minuteOf(firstActivation))`.
  - R9 (#47, Nit) `MatchingWorker.Partition.offer`: no cap or WARN on pending segments per pair (a 24 h replay into a full channel can hold 1440). Add a WARN above a threshold.
- Questions for the author: (1) How will T-048 get the fill price of a LIMIT filled at activation? It is only logged; the plan keeps the limit price. (2) Once NSF-09 exists, a plan whose expiry passed during downtime can be EXPIRED by the job before the replay fills it at an earlier candle; the same applies to a cancel made during the replay. Should replay hold expiry/cancel for that pair, or is the divergence accepted? (3) Should `MinuteKlineFeed` (lifecycle on ApplicationReadyEvent, injects `job.MatchingWorker`) live in `trading.job`? (4) R1: replay the runtime hole inside T-043, or a follow-up task?
- Not decided here: Q-34 (replay window) stays OPEN.
- Next: author answers R1/R2 on #48; #47 can merge after R3 is answered (fix now or follow-up)

  derivatives the same on both; a component with missing inputs is null; `dominantSide` (Futures only) from the

### 2026-09-26 – T-026 Swing support/resistance detector
- Result: **DONE** on `feature/T-026-swing-support-resistance` (2 commits, not pushed, no PR yet)
- Summary: `market.calculator.SwingSupportResistance.nearest(List<StoredCandle>)`, pure Java: over the last 200 closed
  candles, a swing low (high) is strictly lower (higher) than the two candles on each side; the last two candles and the
  first two of the window are never swings; support = highest swing low below the last close, resistance = lowest swing
  high above it, `null` when none (so also for fewer than 5 candles). `BigDecimal` compared with `compareTo` (TD 5.4),
  no rounding (T-028). Result `Levels(support, resistance)` carries prices only, no bias. TD 7.3 gained an
  "Implementation (T-026)" paragraph (window includes the unconfirmed candles; neighbours must lie inside it).
- Branch: `feature/T-026-swing-support-resistance`
- Files: `backend/.../market/calculator/SwingSupportResistance.java`,
  `backend/src/test/.../market/calculator/SwingSupportResistanceTest.java`

### 2026-09-25 – PR #13 merged; D-50; Swagger UI checked; T-102 flaky stream shard test
- Result: **DONE** — PR #14 open, CI green; not merged
- PR #13 merged (`319a240`, CI green). D-50 PROPOSED: market data, futures metrics included, stays public; Q-19 closed
  (docs `ea05364`).
- Swagger UI in `dev`: `/swagger-ui.html` 302 → `/swagger-ui/index.html` 200; its seven static files (css, js, favicons,
  initializer) 200; `/v3/api-docs/swagger-config` 200 (`configUrl`), `/v3/api-docs` 200. Nothing blocked, so no
  `fix/swagger-ui-static` branch.
- Root cause, route case: `BinanceStreamShard.opened()` sets `current` under its lock and calls `listener.onConnected`
  after releasing it; the test waited for `isConnected()` and asserted on the listener's list inside that window (AssertJ
  then printed the list already filled: "expected [false] … [false]").
- Root cause, failed-renewal case: the same window (the old connection is closed before the listener hears of the new
  one, and the test waited for that close), plus a 200 ms renewal on a real scheduler that could renew a third time.
- Evidence: with the test listener delayed 50 ms, the old test fails both cases every time, the new one passes both. Not a
  production race: the listener is called outside the lock by design and no caller relies on the order.
- Fix (test only): wait for the callback asserted on; the renewal case on `ManualScheduler` (virtual time) through the
  shard's existing `ScheduledExecutorService` parameter. No injection point added, no timeout, retry or assertion changed.
- Failure rates: before — route 0/50 in one JVM, 2/5 fresh-JVM runs earlier under load, 0/10 later; renewal 1/50; full
  suite 5/5 green now (each case had failed once in earlier full runs). After — 0/200 isolated each; 10/10 full suites
  (1150 tests each).
- Same pattern elsewhere (reported, not changed): `BinanceStreamShardTest` `NSF03_aDroppedConnection…`,
  `NSF03_aCloseFromTheExchange…` (await `isConnected` then assert `openings`); `NSF03_renewal_opens…`,
  `NSF03_aSilentConnection…`, `NSF03_aConnectionThatKeepsTalking…` (real 200–300 ms timers), `…KeepsTalking`,
  `…closingTheShard…`, `…startingTwice…` (`Thread.sleep`); `ClosedCandlePipelineTest` (elapsed wall time < 100 ms).
- Tests: 1150/1150 locally and in CI (no count change); coverage 99.50% line / 94.91% branch (measured).
- Decisions: **D-50** PROPOSED. Open questions: Q-19 closed.
- Next: T-022.
- Commits: `318878a` test(market): make BinanceStreamShardTest deterministic (T-102).
